The runner moves a ticket's stage on the tracker through the Writer (`writer::VisibleStage`, `Writer::set_stage`, the floor's `SetVisibleStage`), to the state the project maps under `[tracker].states`: `working` once `do` has dispatched or an answer resumed Build (`continue`, once the RESUME is kept); `needs_input` once a round's QUESTIONS or a RE-ASK is posted, before the ticket ref keeps it, so a posted question always shows, and again on a re-ask in case a person moved the ticket meanwhile; `review` once the pull request is open, before its head and checks are read. `VisibleStage::after` names the core event behind each move, and the stand-in driver moves the stage after the same events through the same Writer. A counter-question keeps the ticket waiting. A park moves the stage to `parked` once the PARKED comment is posted, or tried, also when keeping the parked state failed (OWL-148, decided 2026-10-04, architecture section 4): the state the optional `[tracker].states.parked` names, else the `needs_input` one; `continue` restarting a parked ticket moves it, once the restart is kept, to `VisibleStage::of` the status it lands on, `needs_input` or `working`. A move is a `tracker_write` event (`action` = `stage`, `stage`, `state`); a failed one is a `warning` (`what` = `stage_not_moved`, `stage`, `state`, `reason`) and never stops the command or the delivery. The Markdown tracker rewrites the `stage` line. The Linear adapter reads the workflow states of the issue's team (50 at most, refusing more), takes the one named exactly as configured, refusing none or several, and calls `issueUpdate` with its id, checking that the issue answers in that state (C4 above, "Moving the visible stage"). The conformance suite moves the stage on both adapters (`check_stage`); `owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand.rs` follows a round, a re-ask, the resume and the delivery, and a tracker refusing every move; a park at the re-ask limit shown as the project's parked state until its restart, one at Build shown as needs input until `continue` shows it working, and a refused park move.
- **Owlshift's identity on Linear** (OWL-157, decided 2026-10-04; decision D8, C4 above). Optional: with the Linear app's client ID and secret in the keychain (accounts `linear-app-client-id` and `linear-app-client-secret`, per project since OWL-308, part K of "P5 · Quality pipeline"), `do`, `continue` and `watch` request one token per command when they open the tracker, before any work (`client_credentials`, scopes `read,write`, fixed since asking another set revokes every token of the app). They check it with one request through each credential: an app user, of the key's workspace, that sees the project's team (an app reaches public teams only, and another workspace's app would write to its own issue of the same identifier). Every write, comments and stage moves, then goes with that token as a `Bearer` (`linear::app::AppTransport`); reads, mentions included, stay on the API key, which stays required. A 401 gets one new token and one more try, a transport error none; the token is revoked when the command ends, and `watch` keeps one for its life. An app that cannot be used refuses the command, with no fallback to the key, which would hide that Linear no longer tells the decider. Without an app, Owlshift writes through the key as before, and `owlshift doctor` warns; with one, doctor runs the same check, then revokes its token. The adapter reads `user.app`: an app user, Owlshift's own or another integration's, is never an account, so never the decider, and Owlshift's own comments read as Owlshift's by their marker and the ticket ref, as before. QUESTIONS, RE-ASK and REPLY carry `Waiting for <mention>` after their header (`Writer::mentioning`, `Tracker::mention`): on Linear, the profile link of the decider recorded on the ask (`User.url`, read at each post, left out when it cannot be read, which is reported as a `warning`, `what` `mention_dropped`, since OWL-170), which Linear makes a mention; REPLY joins the ticket's "question and re-ask" since it too waits for the decider's answer. Every other comment mentions no one, and still reaches the issue's subscribers (scenario S6). Issue creation (S13) is not built; C4 above tried it. Tests: the adapter's routing, checks, mention and token lifecycle on fakes; the Writer's mention; a bench round whose QUESTIONS, RE-ASK and REPLY mention the recorded decider though the ticket was reassigned meanwhile; `init`'s pair; doctor's `tracker identity` line.
- **Who runs it.** `owlshift continue`, and in the scenarios the test bench's stand-in driver, which keeps its asks, their verdicts, the resolver's decisions and Build's refusal in memory as the ticket ref's `TicketQuestions` would (OWL-126: `TicketQuestions::keep_check` and `round_asks`, `answer_check::readiness` with no quiet window, `on_demand::thread` and `account_author`, so the bench tests the runner's own loop and not a copy), runs the role and posts the re-ask, the REPLY, the RESUME and the PARKED comment with the same pieces, and moves the visible stage as `continue` does (below). `tests/scenarios/reask.toml` plays two question rounds: an incomplete answer re-asked with the open question alone, a counter-question answered by a REPLY with no Build run, a check refused for a verdict on another ask, and a RESUME after each round's last answer, round 1's restating Q1 from the first check. `crates/owlshift-testkit/tests/scenarios.rs` parks a ticket at the re-ask limit and after a second failed run, each with its PARKED comment and its stage: the parked state the re-ask project names, needs input for the other. It also checks that a counter-question a check read is not an answer again, and that a decision and an ask take their comments' places in the thread, a decision before the round it leaves.
- **`owlshift continue TICKET` and the quiet window** (OWL-127, decided 2026-10-03). `owlshift resume TICKET` (OWL-122, the entry above) is renamed `owlshift continue TICKET`, with no alias: `resume` names only the background service's dispatch command beside `pause` (P7, [runtime and operations](runtime-and-operations.md#observability--the-local-web-ui)), so each name means one action. The two OWL-122 entries above ("`owlshift do` asks on the ticket" and "`owlshift resume TICKET`") keep the name they were written with; read `continue` there. Comments already posted on tickets keep the old name; the dispatch event of the renamed command records `command` = `continue`, free-form `data` that no reader parses, so the event format stays 2. Once answers arrive, `continue` checks them only when the decider's reply counts ([architecture](architecture.md), section 4, "No premature resume"): `answer_check::counts_at` takes the decider's comment with the newest last edit (on a tie, the one created last, then the last the tracker lists) and asks `owlshift_core::reply::counts` whether it was left unedited for the quiet window (`policy.quiet_window_minutes`, default `DEFAULT_QUIET_WINDOW`, 10 minutes) or ends with `go` (`reply::ends_with_go`: its last word, punctuation trimmed, in any ASCII case). The time it was left unedited is this machine's clock (`OnDemand::clock`) less the tracker's last edit, the one place a tracker time meets this machine's; a last edit after the clock counts as none, so the whole window applies from that edit. Too early, `continue` stops with `Stop::Settling`, which says when the reply counts and that `go` makes it count now, and keeps nothing of the check; a parked ticket is restarted first, and that restart is kept, as before. The window is the project's `policy.quiet_window_minutes` (OWL-145, decided 2026-10-03): whole minutes from 1 to 1440, 10 when absent, refused at config load outside the range; 0 is refused because every edit would count at once, which `go` is for, and a day is the longest a ticket is worth holding for an answer to settle. `counts_at` takes the window, `Stop::Settling` carries it for its message, and `watch` passes the same value (OWL-152, below). `owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand.rs` stops a `continue` typed within the window, with no run and the ticket ref unchanged, checks the same answer at the time it counts, delivers on an answer ending with `go`, and holds a parked ticket's restart through the window.
- **Late comments before delivery** (OWL-139, decided 2026-10-04), architecture section 4's "No premature resume" and scenario S2 step 5. In `OnDemand::build`, for a ticket whose ticket ref keeps an ask, a Build run that is done with its gate passed is not delivered yet: the runner reads the comments again and keeps the late ones (`answer_check::late_comments`: the latest ask's decider's, not marked, last edit newer than the newest decider edit the run's brief held, which a comment of the same time misses, as `new_answer` does). None: delivery as before. Some: a `gate` event (`late_comments`, the comment ids, `outcome` = `integrate`) and one more Build run, from the same state at Build and with the comments read again for its brief; a decision `Late comment` with the comments' times as its basis joins the delivery report; the build role integrates a decider comment newer than the latest RESUME comment, even with every step done, or asks about it (`roles/build.md`, "Resuming"). That run's questions go to the decider without the resolver, as round N+1, its summary prefixed with the late comments' times; a failed run, a usage limit or a breach goes as for any Build run. When it is done, a further late comment (`outcome` = `ask`) opens round N+1 with one `scope` question built by the runner (`on_demand::late_round`), which names the comments' times and asks whether the work takes them into account before delivery; nothing is pushed. The bound counts per command: a `continue` after a usage limit integrates again. A comment the tracker refuses to list stops with `Stop::Delivery`, the ticket left at Build. `owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand.rs` integrates a late comment into a second Build run and its pull request, a comment by someone else during that run counting for nothing, stops in needs-input when the integrating run asks, and stops after one integration run.
- **`owlshift watch`** (OWL-152, decided 2026-10-04): the decisions are architecture section 5's "Watch mode". `owlshift_runner::watch` holds the loop and its pure bookkeeping (`Holds`: the 10-minute hold of a continue that left the ticket ref as it was, the pause until a usage limit's reset); `ticket_ref::list` lists the ticket refs; `answer_check::readiness` decides, for watch and for `continue`'s answer check alike, whether the decider's reply counts; `OnDemand::continue_seen` is `continue` run only while the ticket ref, read under the lock, is still the commit watch saw. Its `decision` event carries `watch` = `continue` and the decider's newest edit (`answer`), and an unreadable ticket is a `warning` with `what` = `watch_unreadable`: free-form data, so the event format stays 2. `owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand.rs` plays the acceptance: a round asked by `do`, then passes that wait, read the comments through a tracker failure, hold a reply within a project's 20-minute window, skip a locked and then a quarantined project, and continue the ticket once its reply counts, to a delivery, with no `continue` typed.
- **Watch after delivery** (OWL-208, decided 2026-10-10): the decisions are those of architecture section 5, "Watch after delivery". Below, who builds what, so that no implementation ticket decides them again. Each pending live check is the first step of its ticket, run before the code that depends on it (AGENTS.md).
  - **OWL-209: following the pull requests.**
    - *`delivery.json`.* A new contract, `TicketDelivery`, with its schema in `schemas/`, kept in the ticket ref beside `state.json`. A ref the delivery makes holds a `questions.json` with no ask. Fields:
      - the pull request's number and link, the branch and the base branch;
      - `pushed`, `base_at_push`, `rebased_onto` and `pushing` (commit and kind: `delivery`, `fix` or `rebase`);
      - `ended`: why (`merged`, `closed` or `head_moved`) and when.
    - *Delivery.* Writes `pushing` before the push and `pushed` once the head is read back, and moves the core state to Watch: Completed at Build, then at Verify, which has no role before P5, then at Deliver.
    - *The pass over followed pull requests.* Watch reads them and adopts a `pushing` head. It ends the following of a pull request merged, closed or pushed to by someone else, and reads the ticket's stage before any run. `do` refuses a ticket whose pull request is followed, and `continue` at Watch does watch's step once.
    - *The decision table* of section 5, a pure function in `owlshift-core` over plain data: the read, the base's checks, the counts and the times. It returns wait, fix, rebase (trivial or not), park (with the reason) or end (with the reason). OWL-112 and OWL-210 run its fix and rebase.
    - *Forge adapter additions.* `HeadMoved` carries the head it read. A query for the checks of a commit gives the base's head and `base_at_push`. `rateLimit { cost remaining resetAt }` is added to each query watch polls; delivery's one-shot queries keep their recorded text (see "Implemented", below). A rate-limited answer is its own error kind, which holds the forge reads until its reset.
    - *Events.* Each change watch reads (verdict, mergeability, leaving the list) and each action it takes is a `decision` event whose `watch` names it: free-form data, so the event format stays 2.
    - *Live checks.* (a) The checks query's GraphQL cost, recorded as a fixture from a real call. (b) A real conflicting pull request read as `CONFLICTING`, recorded as a fixture. Neither could be run from the cloud session that designed this, whose GraphQL calls GitHub refuses; both were run on 2026-10-12 by OWL-217 (below).
    - *Scope moved on 2026-10-11, by the maintainer.* Reading the ticket's stage before a run moves to OWL-112 and OWL-210, since OWL-209 starts no run. OWL-209 decides fix, rebase and park, and records each as a `decision` event; it applies no `FixDue`, `RebaseDue` or `ForgeBlocked` and posts no PARKED comment, which OWL-112 and OWL-210 do. The one state change OWL-209 applies is `ChecksGreen`, when it changes the state. Whether a rebase is trivial is OWL-210's, from `git merge-tree`.
    - *Implemented* (OWL-209, 2026-10-11).
      - `owlshift_contracts::refs::TicketDelivery`, `schemas/ticket-delivery.schema.json`: format 1. `pull_request` is optional, since a delivery's `pushing` is written before its pull request exists. `pushing` keeps `base` beside the commit and kind (architecture section 5, "Owlshift's own pushes"). `branch` and `base_branch` follow git's branch-name rules.
      - `ticket_ref` reads and writes `delivery.json` beside the two other files, and every write carries it forward. `forget` drops it.
      - `owlshift_core::follow::decide` is the table. The runner's `follow` module maps the forge's read onto it (`read_of`), keeps watch's follow memory (`Following`: the forge's pause, the last state recorded per pull request, the first-failure times), and runs the step that watch and `continue` share.
      - The adapter: `GitHubForge::follow` (the pull request's read, without an expected head) and `GitHubForge::base_checks` (the base head's checks and `base_at_push`'s, paged per commit) carry `rateLimit`. Delivery's queries keep their live-recorded text, so their cost is not reported. `ErrorKind::RateLimited` comes with its reset (`Error::resets_at`): a GraphQL `RATE_LIMITED` error, or HTTP 403 or 429 with `x-ratelimit-remaining: 0` or a `Retry-After`. The GraphQL `Response` keeps those rate-limit headers only, never any other, so a recording still holds no credential. `HeadMoved` carries the head read (`Error::head`).
      - Events: one `decision` per followed pull request when what watch reads changes. `watch` is `read` for the first look in a watch's life, else the first change among `ended`, `adopted`, `mergeable`, `verdict` and `action`; beside it are `pull_request`, `head`, `verdict`, `mergeable`, `action`, `base` when the base was read, and `reason` once ended. Warnings are `forge_rate_limited` (once per hold), `watch_forge_unreadable` (once per kind of failure, until a read succeeds) and `checks_overdue` (once per head). The delivery adds `base_head_unread` when it cannot read the base head before its push. A look whose ref change finds the project busy, or the ref moved, writes and records nothing, and the next pass looks again.
      - `owlshift continue` at Watch returns `Stop::Watched`, printed with exit status 0 and no notification.
      - Tests: the table row by row in core; the follow memory in the runner; the adapter on fixtures; on the bench, a pull request going red, then conflicting, then merged (one event each, no read after the merge), `do` refused, and `continue` at Watch.
    - *Checked live on 2026-10-12 (OWL-217).* OWL-209's cloud session could not run checks (a) and (b) either (GraphQL answered HTTP 403, "GitHub GraphQL is not available from Claude Code sessions"), so its fixtures of watch's queries were made up; they now replay real answers, recorded from the maintainer's machine.
      - (a) Every query watch polls cost 1 point: the pull request's read red, conflicting and merged, and each base-checks query. The base-checks fixture holds six queries, 6 points, only because it is recorded one check per page to keep the paging covered (3 checks at the base head, 4 at `base_at_push`). At the adapter's page size of 100 the base read is one query, 1 point. Architecture section 5, "Cost", holds.
      - (b) A pull request whose base took a conflicting commit read `CONFLICTING` and `DIRTY`, after `MERGEABLE` and `UNSTABLE` while only a check failed. A merged one reads `UNKNOWN` for both.
      - `records_watch_reads` (`github_live.rs`) records `follow-red.json` and `follow-conflicting.json` on a disposable repository with Actions enabled (`pitchopp/owlshift-live-sandbox`; never `pitchopp/owlshift`, which the test refuses). Its branch adds a workflow with a passing and a failing job; it pushes a commit to the repository's `main` to make the conflict, then closes the pull request and deletes its branch. `record_watch_fixtures` (`github.rs`) records `follow-merged.json` (PR #16) and `base-checks.json` read-only on `pitchopp/owlshift`, with `base_at_push` a `main` commit whose CI failed:

        ```sh
        OWLSHIFT_LIVE_GITHUB=<owner>/<test-repo> \
          cargo test -p owlshift-adapters --test github_live -- --ignored --nocapture records_watch_reads
        cargo test -p owlshift-adapters --test github -- --ignored --nocapture record_watch_fixtures
        ```

      - `rate-limited.json` stays made up, and says why: spending the primary budget would drain the credential's budget shared with everything else using it, and a secondary limit cannot be provoked safely. The hygiene test allows that one fixture only.
  - **OWL-55: the core's counts and events.** Its question, a dedicated counter for gate failures, is answered no: the gate keeps `MAX_FAILED_RUNS`, as "The project gate", "Follow-ups" records. Its work becomes the fix-pass and repair counts below; its title is to be changed to match.
    - *State.* `TicketState` gains `fix_passes` (`MAX_FIX_PASSES` = 2) and `repairs` (`MAX_REPAIRS` = 6). `TICKET_STATE_FORMAT` goes from 2 to 3, and a format-2 document reads with both at 0. `restore` refuses either count past its bound, or above 0 at a stage other than Watch.
    - *Events, valid at Watch.* All are added to `Event::ALL`.
      - `FixPushed` and `RebasePushed`: the ticket stays at Watch, the failed runs are cleared, and `repairs`, plus `fix_passes` for a fix, gain one.
      - `ChecksGreen` clears `fix_passes` and the failed runs.
      - `FixDue` and `RebaseDue` park at a bound (`ParkReason::FixPasses`, `ParkReason::Repairs`) and otherwise leave the state as it is.
      - `ForgeBlocked` parks (`ParkReason::Forge`): a red no run fixes, or a push the forge refused.
    - *Existing events.* `ResultRefused` becomes valid at Watch as at Build, and `Restarted` clears both counts.
    - *Doc comments.* The `Event` table's Watch row, `Pipeline::loop_back` and `Stage::default_role` stop saying that fixes and rebases loop back to Build: at Watch, only review comments (P5) loop back.
    - *Implemented* (OWL-55, 2026-10-11) in `owlshift_core::state` (`MAX_FIX_PASSES`, `MAX_REPAIRS`, `TicketState::fix_passes` and `repairs`, the six events, the three park reasons) and `owlshift_contracts::refs::PersistedState` (format 3, reading format 2 with both counts at 0 and refusing a format-2 document that carries either). Three points the design left open:
      - Leaving Watch, which only review comments do (P5), clears both counts, as a new stage clears the failed runs: the next delivery is a new one, and `restore` refuses the counts elsewhere.
      - `FixPushed` with the fix passes or the repairs at their bound, and `RebasePushed` with the repairs at theirs, are invalid transitions: the runner gives `FixDue` or `RebaseDue` before each run.
      - `FixDue` with both counts at their bounds parks for the fix passes.
  - **OWL-112: the fix run.**
    - *The brief's `check_failure`.* A new brief format, with its schema, filled with the failed checks and their excerpts. The job log is read through REST `actions/jobs/<id>/logs` by the runner, the forge's only credential holder, which follows the redirect without the token. Timestamps and ANSI escapes are removed, the 16 KiB ending with the first `##[error]` line are kept, and the brief's 64 KiB limit applies.
    - *`roles/build.md`.* What a fix run is, and that a failure outside the branch is asked about.
    - *The run.* The build role at Watch on the Build routing, the gate, then the leased fast-forward push, a new DELIVERY report, `FixPushed`, and parks through `FixDue` and `ForgeBlocked`. Before the run, the ticket's stage is read (architecture section 5, "A ticket a person moved"), moved here from OWL-209. OWL-112 also parks on the table's non-code red (`ForgeBlocked`), which OWL-209 only records.
    - *Pending check.* (c) A fine-grained token needs "Actions: read" to download a job log, checked with the maintainer's token; `owlshift init`'s help for the GitHub token says so.
    - *Implemented* (OWL-112, 2026-10-11); the rules as built are architecture section 5's "The fix run", "As built (OWL-112)".
      - Contracts: `Brief::check_failure` (`CheckFailure`, `FailedCheck`, `BaseState`), brief format 12, `schemas/brief.schema.json`; every role's front matter says 12.
      - Adapter: the follow query reads each check run's `databaseId` and each status's `description` (`Check::id`, `Check::output`); `GitHubForge::job_log_excerpt` streams `actions/jobs/<id>/logs` and keeps the excerpt, following the redirect with an agent that holds no token; `GitHubForge::check_run_output` reads another app's output by REST; `Transport::get` carries both, refused by default. `push_command` takes the lease.
      - Runner: `fix` assembles the brief's `CheckFailure`; `OnDemand::repair` (and `repair_seen` for watch) reads again under the lock, reads the stage (`Tracker::stage`, by the state's name on Linear and the `stage` line on the Markdown tracker), applies `ForgeBlocked` or `FixDue`, runs the build role at Watch through `run_build_once`, which `build` shares, pushes with the lease, applies `FixPushed` and posts the DELIVERY report naming the pass. `follow::changed` applies `FixPushed` when it adopts a `fix` push. Watch holds a repair as a continue (`Holds`). `GITHUB_TOKEN_HELP` names actions (read).
      - `roles/build.md`: "A fix run".
      - *Live check, 2026-10-11 (OWL-220).* The stage query (`query TicketStage($id: String!) { issue(id: $id) { state { name } } }`) was run against the Owlshift workspace: OWL-112 answers `{"data":{"issue":{"state":{"name":"Done"}}}}`, and an unknown ticket answers HTTP 200 with `data: null` and an `errors` entry `Entity not found: Issue` (code `INPUT_ERROR`). Both are recorded in `crates/owlshift-adapters/tests/fixtures/linear/read-stage-owl-112.json` and replayed by `the_linear_tracker_reads_a_tickets_stage`; `record_read_stage_fixture` records it again. The unit test on a made-up answer is gone.
      - Tests: the excerpt, the redirect and the follow fields in the adapter; the leased push against a bare remote; the check-failure assembly and its 64 KiB in the runner; on the bench, red to a fix pushed, green, two more passes and the park; a moved ticket left alone, then a fix run that asks, parked; a red no run fixes and a push the forge refuses, parked; a person's push during the run, which ends the following.
      - *Deviation until OWL-211, removed.* A fix run's questions parked the ticket (`Blocked`) with a PARKED comment listing them; since OWL-211 they open a round returning to Watch.
      - *Check (c) still pending (2026-10-11).* This session had no personal token: its GitHub App user token read #89's check runs (200) and got the 302 on `actions/jobs/110572493177/logs`, as on 2026-10-10. The maintainer runs, with the stored token:

        ```sh
        OWLSHIFT_GITHUB_TOKEN=<the stored token> OWLSHIFT_LIVE_GITHUB=pitchopp/owlshift OWLSHIFT_LIVE_GITHUB_JOB=110572493177 \
          cargo test -p owlshift-adapters --test github_live -- --ignored --nocapture reads_a_failed_jobs_log
        ```

        Then the dated result goes into architecture section 5 and here. Until then a token refused the log makes the repair stop before any run (architecture section 5, "Failing closed on the token").
    - *Follow-up: OWL-218 (2026-10-11).* A DELIVERY report the tracker refused after the push landed, or the report of a push adopted after a crash, which OWL-112 never posted, is now owed in `delivery.json` (format 2) and posted at the next watch pass or `continue`, once: architecture section 5, "The fix run", "The owed report". Rebase pushes share it, and since OWL-146 correction pushes (format 3), each with its own arm in the adoption's count, `post_owed` and `push_what`, so a correction counts no fix pass and its report's lead line, "Corrected after delivery", is told apart from a fix pass's. The push under way keeps the run's summary, gate, checks and follow-ups (`KeptReport`), so an adopted push, and a push sent again after no verdict, reports what the run said. `OnDemand::post_owed_report` is called from `Watch::follow` and `settle_owed_report` from `watch_once`, one line each, after the look is kept and before any repair. Tests: the contract's format-1 read and refusals; `Writer::post_repair_report` posting once per push; adoption owing the report in `follow::changed`; on the bench, a refused report posted at the next watch pass and not again after a crash that lost the ref's write, `continue` posting it and an end dropping it, and an adopted push reported with what its run kept. Since OWL-231 (2026-10-12) the first delivery's report and a correction's with nothing pushed are posted once by the same rule, their lead line (none, for the first) and pull request line, instead of a body identical to the newest report; `post_repair_report` is now the same function under its OWL-218 name. Tests: a first report posted once per head whatever its checks, never stood in for by a repair's on the same head; and on the bench, a delivery whose ref was rewound to its push under way, its checks now read red, posting no second report. Since OWL-236 (2026-10-12) the first delivery's report and a correction's that pushed nothing are owed from the ref write that makes them due (`delivery.json` format 4), and every report goes through `post_owed` and `post_and_settle`: architecture section 5, "The owed report". Tests: the contract's format-3 read and refusals; on the bench, a first report refused at `do` posted once at the next watch pass and not again by `continue` after a crash that lost the ref's write, and the same for an unchanged correction's through `continue`. Since OWL-238 (2026-10-12) checks read at a moved head (`forge::ErrorKind::HeadMoved`) post nothing: `settle_owed` leaves the report owed and ends the pass, or stops `continue`, before any repair, and `post_and_settle` stops before the post, which replaces the stop OWL-236 gave the first delivery alone: architecture section 5, "The owed report". Tests, on the bench: the head moving before the owed report's checks are read, the report held, warned of once across a refusal before and after, then dropped when the next read ends the following; a fix report held after its push; and checks that cannot be read for another reason still posting it.
    - *Moved stage recorded once* (OWL-219, 2026-10-11). `stage_moved` is recorded when the stage read changes, not at each repair attempt; watch's memory (`Following`) keeps it, so a restart records it again. Architecture section 5, "A ticket a person moved".
  - **OWL-210: the rebase run.**
    - *`roles/rebase.md`.* `Role::Rebase` already exists in the core; only its prompt is new.
    - *Classification.* By `git merge-tree`, then the fast tier and its escalation to the Build routing. `Snapshot::take_rebase` is wired in, and the gate runs on the rebased commit. The ticket's stage is read before the run, as for OWL-112 (moved from OWL-209), and `RebaseDue` is applied before it.
    - *The forge adapter's leased forced push.* The one forced push, a new forge capability. A refused push ends the following or parks.
    - *Triggers.* The refresh onto a base gone green, and the failed run for a conflict still read against `rebased_onto`.
    - *Implemented* (OWL-210, 2026-10-11); the rules as built are architecture section 5's "Conflicts and the rebase run", "As built (OWL-210)".
      - Contracts: `Brief::rebase` (`RebaseBrief`: the pull request, the branch, its head, the base branch, `onto`, `refresh`, the conflicting paths), brief format 13, only in the rebase role's brief; `Pushing::onto`, kept for a rebase's push only (the delivery stays format 1); `rebased_onto` is the base the last landed rebase was onto.
      - `roles/rebase.md`: rebase onto the commit `onto`, never a branch name, without `--update-refs`; resolve keeping both sides' intent; abort before asking or failing, never end mid-rebase; the gate before `done`; never push.
      - Adapter: `Pushed::Forced`, read only by `read_forced_push_parts`; `read_push` still refuses a forced update.
      - Runner: `rebase::classify` (the merge-tree rule, a pure `parse` over its `-z` output); the executor's `take_rebase` for a brief with a `rebase`, refused unless its `onto` is the run's base, and the gate after a rebase `done`; `OnDemand::repair_rebase` (`RebaseDue`, the re-push, the classification, the fresh worktree per attempt, the tiers), `rebase_failed`, and `repair_runs`, the run loop fix and rebase runs share; `push_repair`, the fix's push generalised, with `Writer::push_rebased` and `writer::rebase_keeps_delivery`; `follow::landed` writes `pushed`, `base_at_push` and `rebased_onto` for a push that lands or is adopted. `OnDemand` gains `rebase` and `rebase_fast`; `owlshift do`, `continue` and `watch` run the rebase role on the standard tier's model and on `[models].fast`, else the standard one.
      - Tests: the rule on recorded output, the forced reading, the adoption, the report line and the brief's validation in their crates; against a bare remote, the forced leased push, its refusals before git (the base, no change, not on the base, a delivered file lost) and the lease refusing a moved or deleted branch, never recreated; the executor refusing another base; on the bench, a trivial conflict rebased on the fast tier and force-pushed by the runner, then read conflicting against the same base twice and parked; a modify/delete conflict on the Build routing at once, with the base moving during the run; and a rebase failing on the fast tier, then on the Build routing, parked with the branch back at the last push.
  - **OWL-211: back to the decider.**
    - *Questions.* A fix or rebase run's questions go to the decider without the resolver, in a round whose return stage is Watch.
    - *The visible stage.* After an answer at Watch it shows review, not working.
    - *After the RESUME.* The ticket waits for watch's next pass, or a `continue`, which decides by the table. The run that follows carries the round in its brief's thread.
    - *Implemented* (OWL-211, 2026-10-12); the rules as built are architecture section 5's "Back to the decider", "As built (OWL-211)".
      - Runner: `OnDemand::repair_runs` opens the round through `ask` (`Round::of`, no resolver) for fix, rebase and correction runs; `check_answers` gives the RESUME its return stage (`ResumeComment::return_to`) and shows `VisibleStage::of` the resumed status; `VisibleStage::of` shows Watch as review; `ask`'s not-kept message names `owlshift continue` at Watch; watch keeps a followed ticket's hold across passes.
      - Tests: the RESUME's Watch lead and `VisibleStage::of` in the writer; on the bench, a fix run that asks after one fix pass, answered, then a fix run whose thread holds the round, pushed on the same branch, then green; a pull request green after the answer, with no fix run; a rebase run that asks; a correction that asks, answered, then corrects; a round not kept under watch, posted once.
      - Residual, resolved by OWL-242: a pull request merged or closed while its round waits was not read until the round was answered, since watch read no pull request of a waiting ticket.
  - **OWL-242: a round the pull request outlives** (decided 2026-10-13); the rules are architecture section 5's "Back to the decider", "A round the pull request outlives".
    - *Implemented* (OWL-242, 2026-10-13).
      - Core: `Event::FollowingEnded`, from a round waiting at Watch back to Watch, the counts kept.
      - Runner: `follow::changed` applies it when it ends the following of a waiting ticket; `Following::look_ending` reads the pull request with no base read; `Watch::close_round`, before the comments of a waiting ticket at Watch, moves the stage to review while it shows needs input (`OnDemand::review_after_round`), then writes the ref.
      - Tests: the transition table in the core; `changed` on a waiting record; on the bench, a round left waiting by a red read, then kept while its ref cannot be written, then closed by the merge with an answer posted meanwhile, which starts nothing; a closed pull request whose ticket a person moved to done keeps that stage.
  - **OWL-243: a park the pull request outlives** (decided 2026-10-13); the rules are architecture section 5's "Back to the decider", "A park the pull request outlives".
    - *Implemented* (OWL-243, 2026-10-13).
      - Core: `Event::FollowingEnded` is valid from a park at Watch too, back to Watch with the round, the fix passes and the repairs kept, the re-asks and failed runs cleared.
      - Runner: `follow::ends_with_following` names the statuses an end brings back to Watch, for `follow::changed` and `watch::outlived` alike; watch's pass reads a ticket parked at Watch as a waiting one, never looking at its comments; `Watch::close_round` became `Watch::close_outlived` and `OnDemand::review_after_round` became `OnDemand::review_after_end`, which moves the stage to review while it shows the state the ticket's status set (`VisibleStage::of`); `watch::ended_marks` gives the `ended` event's `parked` and `round` and the line's words.
      - Tests: the transition table and the counts in the core; `changed` on parked records; `ended_marks`; on the bench, a ticket parked at Watch by a red no run fixes, left as it was while its pull request is open, then shown in review once it is merged, `continue` then finding nothing to continue; and, on a project that maps a parked state, a closed pull request moving the ticket from it to review.
  - **OWL-146: the correction run** (decided 2026-10-11), scenario S7; the rules are architecture section 5's "A reversed decision".
    - *Implemented* (OWL-146, 2026-10-11).
      - Contracts: `KeptDecision::reversed` (`Reversal`: `through`, `comment`), `questions.json` format 10, format 9 still read; `PushKind::Correction` and `KeptReport::questions`, `delivery.json` format 3, formats 1 and 2 still read; `Brief::correction` (`Correction`, `ReversedDecision`, a reply cut to 16 KiB) and `ThreadEntry::Decision::reversed`, brief format 14; every role's front matter says 14.
      - Core: `Event::Corrected` at Watch, which clears the failed runs and counts nothing.
      - Adapters: `Comment::parent`, read from Linear's `parent { id }` (fixtures recorded again on 2026-10-11) and from the Markdown tracker's `reply_to`.
      - Runner: `reversal` (which replies reverse which decisions, when they count, what the brief gives); `OnDemand::correct` (and `correct_seen` for watch), in `on_demand/correction.rs`, on `repair_runs` and `push_repair` with `RepairKind::Correction`; Build's delivery keeps the replies its brief held; `Watch::follow` reads the comments of a followed ticket with decisions; the DECISION comment and the DELIVERY report's correction line (`writer::Corrected`); a reversed decision is reported as such (`resolver::reported`).
      - `roles/build.md`: "A correction", and a `reversed` decision is never followed.
      - Tests: the reversal rules in the runner; the reply in both trackers; the formats in contracts; on the bench, a reply under watch to a pushed correction past a stranger's reply and the quiet window, a reply followed before delivery that starts nothing then one the pull request already follows, and a correction that fails then asks and parks.
      - *With OWL-218* (merged first): a correction's push keeps its report and owes it as a fix's does, posted once by its lead line ("Corrected after delivery"); its report counts the decisions it follows the replies to, never a fix pass.
  - **OWL-212** runs the reworded exit gate ([roadmap](roadmap.md) P3).

**The resolver** (OWL-138, decided 2026-10-03), architecture section 4's "Resolver first" and scenario S7. A Build's questions go through it before any reaches the decider, but those that reopen a refused choice (OWL-222) (`owlshift_runner::resolver`, `on_demand::resolve`).

- **Routing.** Each question is routed by its category with `GatePolicy::route` (`resolver::route`): the floor's six tokens, a blank category and the project's `policy.always_human` go to the decider and never to the resolver; the rest go to a run of the `resolver` role (`roles/resolver.md`). A `premise_false` result goes to the decider whole (S8). Routing reads the category Build writes, so a floor-topic question filed under another category still reaches the resolver; the second label (below) is what keeps the resolver from deciding it. Since OWL-222, a question the asking run names as reopening a refused choice goes to the decider too, whatever its category (`resolver::route` takes the run's reopened choices; "A retry asks every refused choice" below).
- **The run.** Read-only, without network, in the ticket's worktree at Build's last commit, on Claude Code with the answer check's harness, model and login (standard tier, section 9), and a 15-minute deadline. Its brief (format 7) lists its questions in `resolve`, under Build's ids and without their category, with the thread, the project's rules and `always_human`, the categories the project adds to the floor (OWL-151, decided 2026-10-04: `GatePolicy::additions`, normalized, once each, those the floor covers left out; Build's brief carries the same list, and its prompt and the resolver's tell each run to file or label a question about one under that category, the resolver to pass it on); it never holds a question Build filed as always-human. It may rest a decision on the decider's ticket and comments, earlier decisions, the rules and what it observes in the worktree; a "verifiable fact" is one it can check there, offline. Other people's comments, the runner's own comments and any text saying what to decide are data. Its result (format 5) gives one resolution per question: `decided`, with its own `category`, the `decision` and its `basis`, or `passed_on`, with a `reason`. The executor refuses a resolution on a question the brief did not give, and the runner logs a decision only on a question it gave the resolver and both labels route there (`resolver::settle`).
- **The second label** (OWL-144, decided 2026-10-04). The resolver labels each question it decides from its text and context, never seeing Build's category, so its label is its own reading and not a copy of the one under suspicion. `GatePolicy::route_decided` sends the question to the decider when Build's category is always-human, when the resolver's is not a token (`floor::is_token`: ASCII lowercase letters and digits, words joined by single `_`), or when it is always-human, whole words counting as for Build's (`risk_of_data_loss` is `data_loss`). Such a decision is refused: no DECISION, nothing kept, and its question joins the round, which does not mention it. A token is required because the matcher reads words: `dataLoss` normalises to `dataloss` and `sécurité` to no floor word, so a free spelling could name a floor topic unseen; a malformed label costs the decider a question, never a wrong decision. Labels that merely differ (`naming`, `layout`) pass: the difference says nothing about the floor. What remains, as architecture section 4 records: a floor-topic question both runs label as something else is decided; the resolver's prompt still asks it to pass such a question on, a request on top of the check and not instead of it; the project's `policy.always_human` additions, though told to both runs (above), are still recognised by a model's judgment, so a question on one that both runs label otherwise is decided; and decisions kept before OWL-144 were logged without the check and stay.
- **Decisions.** Each is posted as a `[owlshift] DECISION` comment (`writer::DecisionComment`): the question without Build's id, which a round's numbering would contradict, the decision, what settles it, how to reverse it, and the resolver's run in the footer. It is then kept in the ticket ref (`questions.json` format 4, `decisions`), one at a time. A post that fails sends its question to the decider: no decision is applied unlogged. A post whose keeping then fails stops the command and names the comment; a later run may post a second DECISION. A kept decision makes the ticket ref even when no round opens, an exception to "makes no ticket ref until a round opens" (`owlshift do` above): `continue` then runs Build at Build, and `do` starts from Ready keeping the decisions. Each kept decision takes the place of its comment in every later brief's thread as a `decision` entry, built from the ref alone: a comment that only looks like a DECISION, whoever wrote it, stays a comment read as Owlshift's, never a decision. Build follows a `decision` as the answer to its question, and a decider comment that says otherwise overrides it, wherever it sits in the thread (`roles/build.md`). The delivery report lists every kept decision among the decisions taken without a human. Deleting the dedicated checkout loses the kept decisions with the asks: their comments stay on the ticket as the runner's plain comments.
- **What is left.** Always-human questions, those that reopen a refused choice, passed-on ones and those whose DECISION was not posted go to the decider as the round, in Build's order, renumbered Q1..Qn as a round must be; the QUESTIONS comment says how many others were decided. The core's `Questions` is applied only then: the ticket stays at Build through the resolver. When nothing is left, Build runs again in the same command, with no stop and no counter moved, and without the quiet window of `continue` (OWL-127), which holds a round's answers only: no reply is awaited. After `MAX_RESOLVED_PASSES` (3, `owlshift_core::state`) such runs in a row in one command, the next run's questions all go to the decider, which architecture section 4 records as the bound on "Resolver first".
- **Fallback.** No other harness can take over yet (P6, and a deviation from section 9's fallbacks): a resolver at its usage limit, a failed one, one without a valid result or past its deadline sends every question to the decider in a normal round, which says why. A refused result is kept for the resolver run that replays its questions when the round does not reach the decider (OWL-191, "A refused resolver result told to its replay" below). A resolver that breaks isolation is quarantined and parks the ticket; its restart runs Build again from its checkpoint, which asks again.
- **Events.** A `gate` event for each routing that reaches the resolver or its bound: `resolver` (`done`, `usage_limit`, `failed` or `pass_limit`), `raised_by` and `run`, and the question ids `given`, `decided`, `to_decider` and, after a run, `passed_on`, `refused` (decisions the second label refused) and `unposted`, the last two also in `to_decider`. A `tracker_write` for each DECISION names its `question`, and the round's `gate` event names Build's ids of the questions it asks (`raised_as`).
- **Reversal** (OWL-146, decided 2026-10-11): replying to a DECISION comment reverses it (scenario S7). Before delivery, the next run reads the reply over the decision; after it, watch starts a correction run (architecture section 5, "A reversed decision"; "Watch after delivery", OWL-146, above). Checked live on 2026-10-11 against the Owlshift workspace: `issue(id: "OWL-146") { comments { nodes { id parent { id } } } }` answered `"parent": null` for a top-level comment; `commentCreate(input: { issueId: "OWL-146", parentId: "5d06b27d-…", body: "[test] …" })` answered `success: true` with `parent { id: "5d06b27d-…" }`; `issue.comments` then listed the reply with that parent; `commentDelete` answered `success: true`, and the list was back to its one comment.
- **Who runs it.** `do` and `continue`, and in the scenarios the stand-in driver's `resolve` step, with the same pieces and without the bound, which is per command. `crates/owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand.rs` covers a decision and a round of the rest through to a delivery, a run whose questions are all decided going on without a stop and continued after a usage limit, a resolver at its usage limit, a refused DECISION, the bound and a breach, a decision the resolver labels `data_loss` refused beside one that stands (OWL-144), and a question that reopens a refused choice sent to the decider beside one the resolver decides (OWL-222); `tests/scenarios/resolver.toml` plays a decision beside a project-added always-human category, then a run decided whole.

**Build takes no decision of its own** (OWL-176, decided 2026-10-07), architecture section 4's "Resolver first". On the P2 exit gate, OWL-174 listed three open points "to be decided on the ticket before building"; its Build run decided all three, on bases such as "an example is most useful when it documents the options", and the delivery report listed them among the decisions taken without a human. The prompt let Build "decide what can be discovered", a looser bar than the resolver's, and a Build decision met neither the routing by category nor the second label, so the floor never applied.

- **The role.** `roles/build.md`: how the work is built is Build's (code structure, names, tests), and the pull request's body says what a reviewer should know. A choice of what the work delivers (its content, behaviour, interface or wording) that the ticket, the thread, the rules or the repository do not plainly settle, and every point the ticket leaves open, is a question, filed under `scope` when its answer changes what the ticket delivers. Following the decider's word, a `decision` entry or a rule is not a decision, and is listed nowhere.
- **The runner.** `RunResult::validate_against` refuses a Build `result.json` that lists `decisions`, whatever its status (`result.json` format 6; the field stays in the contract, which no role fills today). The refusal is a failed run, so nothing of it is accepted or delivered, while its commits stay on the branch; the next Build run of the command is told why (`result_refusal`, "A refused result" below): ask each choice as a question, and leave them open even though the work is committed. A retry that lists decisions again, or a `blocked` run that lists any, is one more failed run, and a second one parks the ticket; since OWL-186, so is a retry that ends `done` ("A retry after refused decisions asks" below). The resolver is then the only one that decides without the decider, and the delivery report's decisions are its kept ones and the runner's note of late comments given to a run (OWL-139).
- **What remains.** The runner sees only what a run reports: a choice a run makes without recording or asking it goes unseen, and the reviewer reads the diff. A retry that drops its refused decisions can no longer end `done` (OWL-186, below), nor ask some and drop others (OWL-193, below). Telling a choice of what the work delivers from one of how it is built stays a model's judgment. A run that would have recorded a choice now stops to ask it, at the cost of a resolver run and a Build run that asks.
- **Rejected.** The prompt alone: nothing outside the run checks it, and OWL-174's run ignored the ticket's own instruction. The resolver reviewing Build's recorded decisions before delivery: a resolver mode to confirm a choice already built, a step between the gate and delivery, a resolver run on every delivery with decisions, and the points asked after the build. A category on each Build decision, floor-labelled ones refused: the label is the run's own, with a reason to choose another.
- **Tests.** `tests/scenarios/open_points.toml` is the acceptance: a decider's ticket lists two open points; a Build run that decides them and is `done` is refused before the gate, with nothing posted; its retry, told why, asks both, and both reach the decider as round 1: whether the example shows every key, under `scope`, and, since OWL-222, where the README lists the example, which the resolver decided before. `decisions_against_the_brief` (`crates/owlshift-contracts/tests/contracts.rs`) refuses a Build result with a decision under every status and passes one without.

**The Claude Code harness adapter** (OWL-14), in `owlshift-adapters::harness::claude`, runs one role with `claude -p` on the user's own login, following the OWL-14 results above.

- **Split with the executor.** `command` builds the invocation for a request (working directory, model, effort, permissions, result path, an optional JSON Schema, an optional dollar cap passed as `--max-budget-usd`, from the personal file's `budget_usd`: a dollar cap on each run of a harness, not on a day's total, whatever the billing, [runtime and operations](runtime-and-operations.md#configuration)); the executor spawns it in its own process group or Job Object and hands the child to `drive`, which writes the prompt on standard input, streams every output line to a callback (the executor's run log) and returns the run. The executor stops a run by stopping the process group; `drive` returns at most two seconds after the child exits, even when a process it started holds the output open.
- **Permissions.** Read-only is `dontAsk` plus an allow rule for the result file alone; write in worktree is `acceptEdits` plus Bash; no network removes the web tools; a browser is refused. A result path that would be a pattern in a rule (anything but ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-` and `/`, or a `.` segment) is refused. C1's guardrail flags are always passed, and no settings file applies, the worktree's own `.claude/settings*.json` included (OWL-53); the agent environment (OWL-22 in the results above) covers what lies outside Claude Code. `Run::mcp_servers` names the servers of the `init` event.
- **Outcome.** Completed needs exit status 0 and a final record with `is_error: false`. Otherwise a usage limit still in force at the end of the stream (a `rate_limit_event` with status `rejected`, or an assistant message with `error: "rate_limit"`, not lifted by a later `allowed` report) makes the run a usage limit, with the reset time and window of the rejection when there was one, never read from the message text. Anything else is a failure: no final record, no `is_error` flag, a signal, the prompt not delivered, or an error with its API status, terminal reason and error kind. Completed means the harness finished; the role's `result.json` is still validated by the executor.
- **Usage.** Tokens, cost estimate, duration and turns come from the final record, per model too; a run without one has unknown usage, not zero. The last rate-limit report gives the window, its reset time and the five-hour and seven-day utilisation. `apiKeySource: none` means no API key: the run logged in, with the subscription token unless a gateway or provider variable reached it, which the agent environment prevents (OWL-120); any other value fails the run.
- **Tests.** Contract tests replay the scrubbed recordings in `crates/owlshift-adapters/tests/fixtures/claude/` in CI; the usage-limit one is constructed from C7's shapes until a real limit is recorded. A tiny real run is an ignored test, run by hand with `OWLSHIFT_LIVE_CLAUDE=1`.

**The executor** (OWL-15), in `owlshift-runner::executor`, turns one role into one run: `Executor::run` takes a `RunSpec` (main checkout, worktree, branch, base, run directory, brief) and a `Harness`.

- **Worktree and brief.** The ticket's worktree is created on its branch from the base, resolved to a commit first, or reused when it is the root of a linked worktree of the main checkout's repository (the same common git directory, and a top level of its own, not the main checkout's; paths compared once resolved), on that branch, at the commit the branch has there. Anything else refuses the run: a worktree on another branch, a separate clone on the right branch and commit, the main checkout itself. The branch name must be one git takes as is (`check-ref-format --branch`, no leading `-`). The run's own files live in `.owlshift/run/` in the worktree, kept out of git by a `.gitignore` of `*` in that folder, which touches nothing the worktrees share (`info/exclude` is shared): the brief, whose `result_path` the executor sets to `.owlshift/run/result.json`, the result, and the build role's plan and ledger. An earlier agent could write there, so nothing is written through a link: `.owlshift` and `.owlshift/run` must be real directories, each file is removed and then created anew, and the previous result is removed. The caller's run directory, outside the worktree, gets `stdout.log`, `stderr.log` and a copy of the brief.
- **The base** (OWL-51, decided 2026-09-29). The isolation check leaves the remote-tracking refs out (below), so a run can move `origin/main`, or make it or `origin/HEAD` point elsewhere, unseen. The next run does not start there: its caller fetches, then passes `RunSpec.base` as a commit resolved after that fetch, as `owlshift do` does (`project::sync_checkout`, see "The project's rules" under OWL-20). The check is not extended to `origin`'s default branch instead: the runner's own fetches for other runs write remote-tracking refs during a run (see "What P6 holds to"), so a moved ref could not be blamed on the run, and the next fetch resets it anyway. One shape survives the fetch: a remote-tracking branch made symbolic, pointing at another branch of origin, which git follows; `sync_checkout` refuses a default branch in that shape. Checked on 2026-09-29 with git 2.54 on a clone of a local bare repository: `fetch --prune` put back a moved and a deleted `refs/remotes/origin/main` and pruned a planted `refs/remotes/origin/evil`, `remote set-head origin --auto` pointed `origin/HEAD` back at `origin/main` from it, but a `refs/remotes/origin/main` made symbolic to `refs/remotes/origin/topic` stayed symbolic, and `rev-parse` then gave `topic`'s commit. The executor takes `RunSpec.base` as a full commit id or as a remote-tracking name looked up as exactly `refs/remotes/<name>` (`show-ref --verify`), then peeled to a commit; an abbreviated id, a local branch name and a revision expression are refused (OWL-66). Checked on 2026-09-29 with git 2.54: `rev-parse origin/main` gave a planted `refs/heads/origin/main`; `rev-parse refs/remotes/origin/main`, with that ref absent, gave a planted `refs/heads/refs/remotes/origin/main`; `show-ref --verify` refused the absent ref and `origin/main~1`; a full commit id resolved to itself despite a branch of that name.
- **Before the spawn.** `AgentEnv::check` runs in the worktree for the forge hosts, and a finding refuses the run. The caller builds the `AgentEnv` with the project's declared variables (`stack.gate_env`, OWL-44) and the operator's `allow_gate_env` (OWL-63), and names the forge hosts (OWL-20). Whatever fails up to the spawn (worktree, run files, credentials, the first snapshot, the command) is an error of `run`: nothing ran, no run is counted.
- **Spawn and stop.** A `Harness` builds its command and reads its own output; the executor sets the working directory, replaces the whole environment with the agent's, pipes the three streams and spawns the command as the root of a `ProcessTree`. A watchdog thread stops the tree at the executor's deadline, a failed run; the tree is stopped again after every run, before the result is read, so nothing the run left running changes what is checked. `ClaudeHarness` uses the adapter's `command` and `drive`, with the role's prompt followed by one line naming the brief file; an MCP server in its `init` event fails the run. `drive_plain` serves a harness without a driver of its own, such as the fake harness. The executor's own git commands run through `owlshift_platform::process::run_command`, also as the root of a process tree, stopped after 120 s, with their whole output: the probes' 64 KiB cap would cut a large repository's status and blind the isolation check (OWL-48). The watchdog stays for the runs whose output is streamed to a log, the harness and the gate's commands.
- **Outcome**, in this order: an isolation violation quarantines the run, whatever else happened, and the core parks the ticket; then the deadline, an MCP server loaded, a usage limit (an interruption, with its reset time) and a harness failure. Otherwise the result decides: `Finished` needs the harness to report completion and a valid `result.json`, read without following a link and at most 1 MiB, validated against its contract, with `pr.branch` equal to the run's branch and every artifact readable (OWL-26, OWL-35). A harness failure with a valid result is still a failure, and so is an exit status 0 without one.
- **Isolation**, after every run. The main checkout: its HEAD commit and branch, and every entry of `git status --porcelain=v2 --untracked-files=all --ignored=matching` with the content hash of each file it lists (tracked and staged changes, untracked files, ignored ones such as `.env`; an ignored directory counts by its presence). The repository's shared `config`, `config.worktree`, `hooks/` and `info/`, where a planted hook would later run code in the user's checkout. Every ref but the run's branch and the remote-tracking refs: tags, other branches, `refs/stash`. And the worktree: still on its branch, at a commit that descends from the one the run started from, or, for a rebase, from its new base. A check that cannot run counts as a violation. Nothing is written: `--no-optional-locks`, and `hash-object` without `-w`. Before any git runs, two checks read the file system alone (OWL-59): the worktree's `.git` link (see "The worktree's `.git` link" under OWL-20 below), and the shared git files against the snapshot, from the common directory resolved before the run. A violation there ends the check with no git run, so a command a run planted in the shared configuration, such as a clean filter that `git status` of the checkout would run, never runs with the runner's environment. Every command of the runner's git, the executor's and `owlshift do`'s alike, runs with `-c core.hooksPath=<a folder that does not exist> -c core.fsmonitor=false`: no hook and no file-system monitor runs, `worktree add`'s `post-checkout` included. The rest of the runner's environment is kept, since the person's own configuration, such as `safe.directory` or what the fetch authenticates with, must still apply (decided 2026-09-29). Two ways past those checks are closed (OWL-50, decided 2026-09-29), both checked live that day with git 2.54.0. A nested repository in the main checkout, with a clean filter in its own `config`, a `* filter=<it>` attribute and a changed file, and its gitlink staged in the checkout's index (or committed), made the hardened `git status` start git in it and run the filter three times; the main checkout's status now runs with `--ignore-submodules=dirty`, which starts no git in a submodule and still lists a gitlink added or moved, where `all` hides the gitlink too and `untracked` still runs the filter. The monitor stays off in a nested git either way: `-c core.fsmonitor=false` reaches it through `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`. A `commondir` file written in the main checkout's git directory made git there read another repository's configuration, and run its filter; `commondir` is now one of the shared git files, so it stops the check before any git. The check still runs with the runner's environment, not through `Git::as_agent` as the gate's checks do: the agent environment strips credential variables, not the runner's files, which a program that did run would reach anyway; and the snapshot, taken before the run, would need the same environment for ignored files (`core.excludesFile` found through `HOME` or `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`) to compare equal. What that git can still run is what the checked configuration already named, such as a filter driver configured before the run (see the limits below); this settles the check's part of the follow-up under OWL-16.
- **Its limits.** The check assumes nobody else changes the main checkout during a run: a person editing or committing there looks like a breach. `owlshift do` (OWL-20) therefore hangs worktrees off a checkout nobody edits, a clone under Owlshift's data directory, so that a person keeps working in theirs (S12; see "`owlshift do`" below). It does not see changes outside the repository, a file inside an ignored directory, an edit inside a submodule's work tree (OWL-50; the checkout never populates one), an edit hidden by an index flag (`assume-unchanged`, `skip-worktree`), or a process that left the tree with `setsid`; confining an agent is the operating system's job (OWL-41). Its checks before any git trust the configuration they compare to be the whole of it: a file an `include.path` of the snapshot's configuration names, or a change past the first MiB of a shared file, goes unseen, and a filter driver configured before the run still runs on the checkout's changed files, with whatever script it names; the dedicated clone (OWL-20) has none of these.
- **Git's own housekeeping** (OWL-197, decided 2026-10-08). Once a repository holds enough loose objects, `git commit` and `git fetch` start `git maintenance run --auto` in the background, where it outlives them; its `git repack` runs `git update-server-info`, which writes the shared `info/refs`, so a run during which it lands was quarantined for nothing. CI's Ubuntu leg quarantined `refusals_resolver_passes_and_a_late_comment_stop_at_the_ceiling` that way once: the bench's stand-in agent, which runs without the sandbox, had committed. A confined agent cannot write `info/`, but the runner's own `fetch` in `project::sync_checkout`, just before the first snapshot, starts the same maintenance outside the sandbox. Checked on 2026-10-08 with git 2.54.0 (Apple Git-157) on macOS, in scratch repositories: `commit` and `fetch` started it (a geometric or cruft repack, then a multi-pack index) once two loose objects lay in `objects/17`, about 800 in all, not with one there at 400, so whether it fires depends on object ids; `worktree add`, `worktree prune`, `hash-object -w`, `mktree`, `commit-tree`, `update-ref`, `push` and `status` started none. With `-c repack.updateServerInfo=false` on `commit` or `fetch`, the repack still ran and `info/refs` stayed absent; without it, `info/refs` was written. With `maintenance.geometric-repack.auto = -1` added, locally, to the bench's git configuration, that test was quarantined at the same run as on CI (attempt 8, the first whose stand-in commits), and trace2 showed the stand-in's `commit` starting the maintenance, then the repack; with the agent environment's setting below, the same bench passed. Decision: git that Owlshift starts does not update the server information, and the check stays as it is. Every command of the runner's git runs with `-c repack.updateServerInfo=false` beside its hook and monitor settings (`executor::git`), which reaches the maintenance and the repack git starts itself; the agent environment carries the same as a second command-scope entry of OWL-22's overrides (`GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=2`). A confined agent's write was refused anyway, but its git printed `error: unable to update .git/info/refs: Permission denied` (checked with `info/` read-only), which a model may chase. Git knows the setting from 2.36.0 (`builtin/repack.c` at the tags v2.35.0 and v2.36.0, read on 2026-10-08); `owlshift do` already needs a newer git, for `symbolic-ref --no-recurse`, absent at v2.36.0 (new in 2.39.0, the floor `owlshift doctor` checks: OWL-199). The acceptance holds by prevention: a run that writes `info/refs` itself, or whose git ignores the setting, is still quarantined. Not chosen: leaving `info/refs` out of the check, or checking only `info/exclude`, `info/attributes` and `info/sparse-checkout`, which lets a guarded folder change unseen; `maintenance.auto=false`, which would leave Owlshift's long-lived clone with no housekeeping, the runner's fetch and the agents' commits being the only commands that start it there; maintenance in the fetch's foreground, within its 120 s deadline. Tests: `executor::git`'s `the_runners_git_leaves_the_server_info_alone`, where a `gc` through the runner's git writes no `info/refs` and plain git's does, and `tests/isolation.rs`, where a `gc` by the agent's git during a run passes while a `gc` with `repack.updateServerInfo=true` (plain git's until OWL-240 below) and a written `info/exclude` are found. A `gc` stands in for the automatic maintenance: the same repack, run as a child process, on every git version and platform.
- **The bench's own git** (OWL-240, decided 2026-10-12). CI's Ubuntu leg (git 2.55.0) quarantined `a_trivial_conflict_is_rebased_on_the_fast_tier_and_pushed_by_the_runner` once more for `info/refs` ([run 38276043677](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/actions/runs/38276043677), attempt 1, PR #207; the rerun passed). The writer was neither the runner's git nor the agent's, both covered by OWL-197, but the bench's: the test plays the rebase agent itself, inside the run's window, with `GitEnv`, whose configuration lacked the setting. Checked on 2026-10-12 with git 2.54.0 (Apple Git-157) on macOS: trace2 of a scratch rebase shows `git rebase --continue` running a child `git commit`, which starts `git maintenance run --auto --detach`, then `git repack -d -l --geometric=2 --write-midx`, which writes the checkout's `info/refs`; with `repack.updateServerInfo=false` in the bench's file the repack still ran and `info/refs` stayed absent. Reproduced on the bench by adding `maintenance.geometric-repack.auto = -1`, `maintenance.autoDetach = false` and `gc.autoDetach = false` to its configuration, locally (geometric repacking is the default maintenance of this git; an older one may never repack under these settings): without the fix, that test was quarantined at every run with CI's message, and so were `a_non_trivial_conflict_goes_to_the_build_routing_at_once` and, in `tests/isolation.rs`, `a_runs_own_work_passes_and_every_breach_is_found` and `concurrent_runs_pass_and_every_other_ref_stays_guarded`, all of which run the bench's git within a run's window; with it, every test of `owlshift-testkit` passed (`on_demand` 69, scenarios 33, isolation 6). That Ubuntu's git takes the same path is inferred from the same message on the same test, not traced there. Decision: the file `GitEnv::apply` names, the bench's own git's, carries `repack.updateServerInfo=false`; the agent's copy under `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` does not, so the fake harness's git still relies on the agent environment's override, as in production, and the check is unchanged. The runner's git in the bench reads the same file; its own `-c` stays pinned by `the_runners_git_leaves_the_server_info_alone`, which uses a configuration of its own. Not chosen: exempting `info/refs` from the check, even when its content matches the refs, for a write only the bench makes; the agent environment for each step the bench plays inside a run, where one step missed is the same flake and setup commands, whose detached maintenance can outlive them, stay uncovered; `maintenance.auto=false` in the bench, which removes more than the one write. Tests: `git::tests::the_benchs_git_leaves_the_server_info_alone`, where a `gc` through the bench's git writes no `info/refs` and one reading the agent's copy does; with the agent environment's entry removed, `a_runs_own_work_passes_and_every_breach_is_found` fails on `info/refs` (checked on 2026-10-12), so that test still guards the override.
- **Rebase runs and parallel runs** (OWL-49, decided 2026-09-28). The check takes two inputs the executor does not pass yet: the rebase role (P3) passes the first, the scheduler (P6) the second. Since OWL-210 the executor passes the first, for a brief with a `rebase`. A run alone still calls `Snapshot::take` and `Snapshot::check`, whose rules are unchanged.
  - *Rebase.* `Snapshot::take_rebase` names the new base, resolved to a commit when the snapshot is taken: remote-tracking refs are outside the ref check, so a name resolved after the run could be moved by the agent onto any history. After the run the branch must descend from its start, as for every run, or from that base. The start stays acceptable because a rebase that meets a conflict it cannot resolve aborts and escalates, leaving the branch as it was. Whether the run's commits were replayed is not checked: resolving a conflict changes a patch, a commit already upstream is dropped, a reword changes an id, so any such rule would quarantine good rebases. The replayed content is judged like any run's commits, by the gate, the verify stage and the review. Everything else is checked as for any run: `git rebase --update-refs` moves the branches stacked on the run's and is quarantined, so the rebase role runs without it.
  - *Parallel runs.* `Snapshot::check_among` asks the scheduler twice, just before and just after it reads the refs, for the other runs in flight at any time since the snapshot (`Concurrent`): the branches of runs still running, and those of runs that ended, each with the commit its own check verified (`Checked::tip`). A branch running in either answer is left out, its own run's check guarding it, and so is one that only the second answer knows or whose tip changed between the two. A branch that had already ended in the first answer must still be at its tip. The second answer keeps a run that starts during the check from being flagged; the first keeps a run that commits and ends after its branch was read, but before the second answer, from being held to a tip the read could not see. Tags, the stash, every other branch (a parked ticket's included), the shared git files and the main checkout are checked as for a run alone, and a breach there quarantines every run whose check sees it: the check cannot tell which run did it.
  - *What P6 holds to.* The scheduler registers a run before creating its branch. When a run's check returns, the scheduler moves that branch to the ended runs with the check's tip, and nothing touches it until every run that overlapped it has been checked. A run's branch is held to its tip only by the checks that start after its own check returned; the ones already reading leave it out. A run whose check returned no tip is quarantined and stays out of both sets, so any change to its branch is flagged. Branches are created from a commit, as the executor does: made from a remote-tracking name, a branch gets `branch.<name>` entries in the shared configuration. Worktrees stay outside the main checkout, where a new one would show in its status. The runner's own git work for other runs during a run writes only their branches and remote-tracking refs: a fetch that follows tags would write `refs/tags/`.
  - *Its limit.* A run that appends a commit to another running run's branch, without breaking that run's history rule, goes unseen: while both run, that change cannot be told apart from the other run's own work.
- **Checked live on 2026-09-28** with Claude Code 2.1.283, `haiku` at effort `low`, the adapter's flags, under `env -i HOME PATH USER LANG TMPDIR`. With either permission mapping (read-only: `dontAsk` plus `Edit(./.owlshift/run/result.json)`; write in worktree: `acceptEdits` plus Bash), a role read the brief at `.owlshift/run/brief.json`, which git reports as ignored, and wrote `.owlshift/run/result.json`: exit status 0, no permission denial, `apiKeySource: none`, no MCP server. Then `crates/owlshift-testkit/tests/executor_live.rs`, run by hand with `OWLSHIFT_LIVE_CLAUDE=1` from inside a Claude Code session on the maintainer's machine, took a tiny read-only role through the executor: no credential finding, isolation held, `Finished` with the expected summary, in 9 s. C7's open item, the exit status at a real usage limit, stays open: no limit was reached.
- **Tests.** The scenarios run on the executor and the fake harness: the smoke scenario (a usage limit, a crash, questions, a valid build committed and pushed, an invalid result, a valid verify), and three short ones on its fixture in `crates/owlshift-testkit/tests/scenarios.rs`: a file written in the main checkout and a branch switch, both quarantined and parked despite a valid result, and a run stopped at a one-second deadline. `tests/isolation.rs` finds every kind of violation on one repository, after a run's own work (a commit, its ignored files, a fetched ref) passes. It passes a rebase onto the new base and an aborted one, and quarantines a rewrite below both the start and the base with the remote-tracking ref moved, and a rebase with `--update-refs` (git 2.54 locally, then CI's). Two concurrent runs pass, one ending first, while during a check a third starts and a fourth ends at a commit the check's read did not see; the same check told of no other run, an ended run's branch moved, a parked ticket's branch and a tag are still found; `tests/executor.rs` checks the refusals before the spawn: a credential in the repository's configuration, a worktree on another branch, a separate clone and the main checkout given as the worktree, run files reached through a link.

**The project gate** (OWL-16), in `owlshift-runner::executor::gate`. The build role runs `stack.gate` and says so in its result, but a claim is never proof: after every Build run that ends `done` and breaks no isolation, the executor runs the gate itself.

- **How.** Each command of the brief's `gate`, in order, from the worktree root, through the platform's shell: `sh -c` on Unix, `cmd /d /s /c` on Windows with the line passed verbatim. It runs with the agent environment (OWL-22), never the runner's, with no input, as the root of a process tree; the first failure stops the gate. An empty gate runs nothing and passes, which the delivery report states as "the project declares no gate command"; the build role still stops with `blocked` on one, so only a role that ignored that rule or a test bench gets there.
- **On the delivered commit.** The worktree must be clean before the gate (uncommitted changes fail it without running a command), clean after it, and at the same HEAD: a passing gate vouches for the commit the run delivers, which the report records. These checks run git with the agent environment and `core.fsmonitor` off, so a helper the run planted in the repository's configuration never runs with the runner's environment, and their status with every configured filter driver switched off (OWL-73, under "Follow-ups"). The result and its artifacts are read before the gate, so gate code cannot change what the runner acts on.
- **Output and deadline.** The commands' standard output and standard error go interleaved, and whole unless abandoned, to `gate.log` in the caller's run directory; a failure keeps the last 16 KiB, cut on a character boundary. The commands share one deadline, `Executor::gate_timeout` (`DEFAULT_GATE_TIMEOUT`, 30 minutes, until a caller has a reason for another): each gets what is left, none starts once it is spent, and a command running at the deadline is stopped with its tree. The tree is also stopped when its root exits, so a process left holding the output does not hold the gate; the output of one that left the tree is waited for until the deadline, at least 2 s, then abandoned, and the gate fails with the output read until then (OWL-54), which is also what the log has of it. What it writes later is still read, so the process is not stopped by a closed pipe, but dropped: the drain writes the log under a lock that spans each write, and the gate empties it under that lock when it abandons the output, so nothing lands after the command's status line (OWL-69). The checks around the commands keep the executor's own 120 s git deadline.
- **Verdict.** Isolation is checked again after the gate, since the gate ran code the run wrote: a breach quarantines the run, whatever the gate said. Otherwise a red gate makes the run `Failure::Gate`, which the core counts as a failed run (`RunFailed`): the ticket stays at Build, nothing is pushed, and the existing breaker gives one fix run before parking it. The caller puts the failure in the next Build brief (`gate_failure`), where the build role reads its output as data; a passing gate clears it, and `RunReport::gate` carries the passing commands and commit for the delivery report. The failure is kept in memory by the caller until ticket refs persist a checkpoint.
- **Tests.** Unit tests in `gate.rs` run real commands on the three CI platforms: order and first failure, the tail of a long output, the deadline, a process left holding the output, the tail taken while the output is still open, an abandoned drain's log emptied only once its write in progress landed and written no more (OWL-69), the agent environment, and on Windows a line with inner quotes; on Unix, a process that left the tree and holds the output fails the gate with the output read before, and nothing it writes after the gate returned reaches the log, which ends with the lines of a command run next on it (OWL-69), and attributes naming a driver of the repository's configuration and one of the user's run neither in the gate's git, before or after the commands, while a plain `git status` runs both (OWL-73). The scenario `tests/scenarios/gate.toml` is the acceptance: the gate fails after a first Build `done`, the next brief carries the failure, and it passes after the fix run. Short scenarios on its fixture cover a gate refused on uncommitted changes, a gate that commits, and a gate that breaks isolation and fails, which is quarantined.
