As built (OWL-312, 2026-10-15): `ProjectConfig.roles` takes `[roles.<role>]` with a required `rules` list, checked as `stack.rules` is, and `owlshift_runner::rules::project_rules` returns the rules of `stack.rules` and of each role, so that a brief takes `for_role(role)`: the `stack.rules` rules, then the role's. The 32 files are checked when `owlshift.toml` is parsed, the 64 KiB when the files are listed at the base commit, before any is read; both count the default `AGENTS.md` when `stack.rules` is left out. A table with no `rules`, or any other key, makes the file invalid. The `dispatch` event's `rules` lists each source once, `stack.rules` first, then each role's in the order of the roles. A command reads only the tables of the roles it briefs (Build and the resolver in the Build loop of `do`, `continue` and the drain, Build alone in a fix or a correction, none in a rebase, intake and the resolver in an intake pass), so one role's bad table refuses no run of another. The answer check, whose brief carries no rule, takes none.
  - Skills: a project that writes its instructions as skills names their `SKILL.md`, which Owlshift reads as one more rule: taken whole, front matter included, the files it points to not followed, and its `description` never used to choose when it applies, since a named file reaches every run of its role. A skill that applies only to part of the repository waits for path-scoped rules, which come with zones at intake (P6). Claude Code's own skill loading stays off (OWL-53): a skill in the worktree comes from the ticket's branch, and Codex has no skills, while the brief serves both harnesses.
  - Rejected: a catalogue the agent loads skills from on demand, by their `description`, as Claude Code does. It spends less of the brief's room, but needs a tool on both harnesses that reads only files of the base commit, and leaves to a model whether a project's instruction applies; it can come later beside this key. A text appended to the prompt from `owlshift.toml`, which would put instructions in the configuration rather than in files the rules already know how to read and review.
  - What stays open: Claude Code 2.1.284's `init` lists the user's own skills even under `--setting-sources ""` (OWL-77, above); whether a confined run, whose home is unreadable, can invoke one was not checked.
- **The worktree's `.git` link.** An agent owns every file of its worktree, `.git` included: pointed at a git directory of its own, or with its administrative folder's `commondir` pointed there, git in the worktree reads that directory's hooks and configuration, which the rest of the isolation check never sees. So `.git` must be a plain file naming a folder of `<checkout>/.git/worktrees`, whose `commondir` leads back to `<checkout>/.git` and which holds no `config.worktree`; anything else is a breach. Both files are read as git reads them, the whole file less its trailing line breaks, so a trailing space names the same folder for the check as for git, and a file past 4 KiB is refused. The executor checks the link itself (`executor::check_worktree_link`, OWL-59): first in the isolation check, after the run and again after the gate, before any git runs, a breach quarantining the run; and before a kept worktree is reused, before any git runs in it, a breach refusing the run. `do` also checks a kept worktree before the run, under the project's lock, so that a breach marks the project. And the runner never runs git with the person's credentials in a worktree: the push leaves from the checkout, where the worktree's commits are, with `--no-verify`; like every command of the runner's git, it runs no hook and no file-system monitor.
- **The unverified marker.** Before each spawn, `unverified` is written. It is removed once the run's isolation check, the `.git` link included, passed, and rewritten with the violations on a quarantine. While it exists, `do` refuses the project and says how to recover: delete `checkout` and `worktrees` for a fresh clone, or delete the marker. Without it, something a breaking run left in the clone (a hook, a configuration entry) would become the next snapshot's norm and run in the runner's own git. A crash or a Ctrl-C in the middle of a run leaves the marker too.
- **Delivery,** after a Build `done` whose gate passed. It goes through the Writer, which asks the floor before each write:
  - `push_branch` pushes the gated commit (`PushBranch`) from the checkout, with the adapter's `push_command` run by the runner's git;
  - `open_pull_request` opens the pull request, or returns the one already open (`OpenPullRequest`);
  - the pull request's head must then be the pushed commit, read up to five times two seconds apart, since GitHub moves it shortly after a push. A branch someone else moved fails the delivery, and no report is posted;
  - the check set is read once, and the delivery report is posted with the gate's commands.

  The report goes out right after the pull request opens, with the checks as read then; waiting for CI is P3's watch. `blocked` is printed, not posted; since OWL-122, `questions` are posted ("The answer check", above). The visible stage moves to review once the pull request is open (OWL-137, "The answer check", above). Exit status 0 for a delivery, 1 otherwise.
- **Events.** Until the local store exists, events go to one JSON Lines file, `<data>/events.jsonl`, shared by every project, one contract `Event` per line (`Event::render_line`).
  - What is recorded: `dispatch`, `run_started` (with the run directory), `run_ended` (outcome, status, summary or reason, gate verdict and commit, exit code, `elapsed_ms` and `wall_ms`, model, harness version), `usage`, `gate` for a question round, `decision` for a park, `tracker_write` for the push, the pull request and the report, and `warning` (OWL-134) for something left out of a run without stopping it: a malformed `zone:` label the brief dropped, with its `label` and `reason`, one event per label and per run (a Build retry is a run, so it repeats them).
  - Durations: `elapsed_ms` is the executor's monotonic time, which deadlines rely on and which leaves out the time the machine sleeps (macOS); `wall_ms` is the span by the system clock, sleep included, so it is what the operator waited, and it is left out when the clock reads earlier at the end than at the start (OWL-262). Gate records give durations from `wall_ms`.
  - Appends: a separate `events.lock` serializes them across processes, so a reader never waits on the log (a lock on Windows would block reads), and a line torn by a crash gets its line break before the next event.
  - Printing: `do` prints each event as it records it. `owlshift logs` prints them all, `logs TICKET` one ticket's, and `--follow` then prints new complete lines until Ctrl-C.
  - Terminal safety: every printed line is made safe first. A value with a space, a quote or a control character is printed JSON-quoted, and control characters and bidirectional overrides are escaped, so a model, a tracker or a file name cannot drive the terminal.
- **`init`.**
  - The project file: written from a commented template and the flags, checked to parse before it is written, and never overwritten. An existing file's tracker decides which secrets are needed.
  - The secrets: the Linear key for a Linear tracker, and the GitHub token and the agent runs' Claude Code token always (OWL-94: `do` runs Claude Code), asked for only on a terminal with hidden input (`rpassword` 7.5). The token's help names `claude setup-token`. Stored ones are kept unless `--replace-secrets`. A secret that is not one word, holding a space or a control character, is not stored, and `init` exits 1. On a non-terminal, `init` names each missing secret and how to provide it, and exits 1: a machine set up before OWL-94 now lacks the token until `init` runs once more on a terminal. Then, for a Linear tracker, the Linear app's client ID and secret (OWL-157), optional and together (`init::store_app_credentials`): an empty client ID skips the app, a client ID without its secret or either one not one word stores neither and exits 1, a stored pair is kept unless `--replace-secrets`, and half a pair left in the keychain exits 1. Since OWL-308 the tracker and forge secrets are the project's, and an empty answer keeps a shared one in use (part K of "P5 · Quality pipeline").
  - Not in P1: stack detection (P9), a check that the tracker answers, daemon registration (P7), and the harness login check, which is `owlshift doctor`'s.
- **This repository** has its own `owlshift.toml`, written by `init`: Linear team OWL, and CI's three commands as its gate. A CLI test holds the gate to `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.
- **Not run live here.** No real `do` was run: the three-ticket exit gate below is the maintainer's. The hidden prompt was not tried on any platform, and the shape of the `createPullRequest` answer still comes from OWL-17's made-up fixture.
- **Known limits, and the follow-ups they call for.**
  - The runner's git commands keep the executor's 120 s deadline (`executor/git.rs`), which can cut the first clone of a very large repository (OWL-60).
  - `logs` shows where each run's logs are, not their content.
  - Worktrees are not removed after a merge.
  - On macOS the data directory's path holds a space (`Application Support`), which some project tooling mishandles.
- **Tests.**
  - `owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand.rs` plays `do` on the Markdown tracker, the fake harness, a bare remote and the real GitHub adapter over a fake transport. The agent runs bare (`without_confinement`), as the scenarios' isolation tests do: a breaking agent must reach what the sandbox closes, and confinement has its own tests. One confined `do` checks that a machine or a harness that cannot run a confined agent is refused before anything is cloned. The runs take a lock of their own, one at a time: a child forked by another thread shares every open file until it execs, a just-released project lock included. It covers a delivery and its retry: the fetch, a push up to date, the pull request and the report found, not made twice. It covers a red gate fixed on the second run, which resumes from the plan with a lagging pull request head. It covers every stop before a delivery: questions, blocked, a usage limit, a quarantine, two red gates, a refused push, a branch moved after the push, and a ticket with no decider, refused before anything is cloned when it declares no zone. A ticket without an assignee and labelled `zone:docs` is decided by that zone's owner from `do` to the delivery: its asks record the owner, a comment of the person who wrote the ticket is no answer, even after the label is removed, and the owner's is; an owner named only in the person's unpushed `owlshift.toml` decides nothing. A quarantine keeps the project refused. An agent that points its `.git` at a copy of the checkout's git directory, with a `pre-push` hook and the `core.hooksPath` that runs it, is quarantined by the executor: the hook never runs and nothing is pushed. `tests/executor.rs` has the executor quarantine a run that redirects its link from the harness or from the gate, with no runner git run while the link is redirected, refuse a kept worktree whose link was redirected, and run neither a `post-checkout` hook nor a file-system monitor the checkout's configuration names, which plain git runs; `tests/isolation.rs` has a redirected link, and a clean filter planted in the shared configuration, stop the check before any git, the filter never running where `git status` runs it, and so does a `commondir` written in the checkout's git directory; a nested repository with its own clean filter and monitor, its gitlink staged in the checkout, is found as a changed path with neither run, where plain `git status` runs the filter (OWL-50). A new default branch on the forge becomes the base at the next fetch. A run that moves `origin/main` and `origin/HEAD` is not a breach, but the next branch starts from the forge's `main`, even with a local branch named `origin/main` at the run's commit; a default branch made symbolic is refused (OWL-51).
  - `owlshift-testkit/tests/rules.rs` reads the rules on one repository, a commit per case: a regular file, none, a symbolic link, a folder and a submodule, the 64 KiB limit and one byte over, other bytes than UTF-8, a ticket branch and a working tree that changed the file, and a local branch named like the base. With `stack.rules` parsed from an `owlshift.toml` (OWL-67): named files in the order named, a blank one skipped, a ticket branch that changed one, `[]`; a named file missing, under a symbolic link or inside a submodule, and a named folder; two files at 64 KiB together and one byte over. The contract tests refuse each bad path and keep `[]` apart from a left-out key. In `on_demand.rs`, the fix run's brief carries the seeded `AGENTS.md`, and one over the limit on the forge's default branch refuses `do` before any event.
  - Unit tests cover the event log, the project folders and lock, the origin checks, the thread's relations, the template and the secrets. Every secret test uses an in-memory keychain.
  - CLI tests cover `init`, `do`'s refusals before the keychain, and `logs` with `--follow`.

## P0 tasks & exit gate

Each task is an issue in the Owlshift Linear workspace (team `OWL`), with its blockers recorded as relations.

- [x] Create the repository with its licence (2026-09-27)
- [x] OWL-5 · Set up the Owlshift Linear workspace: team `OWL`, states including Needs Input and Triage, labels, one project per step (2026-09-27)
- [x] OWL-6 · Run checks C1, C3, C6 and C7 (Claude part) and record the results (2026-09-28)
- [x] OWL-7 · Settle decision D10 (contribution terms: DCO, 2026-09-28); add the contribution guide
- [x] OWL-8 · Cargo workspace with the six crates; CI matrix on macOS, Linux and Windows (format, lint, tests) (2026-09-27)
- [x] OWL-9 · Write the contracts and generate their JSON Schemas (2026-09-28)
- [x] OWL-10 · Core: model, state machine, scheduler skeleton, policy floor, with unit tests (2026-09-28)
- [x] OWL-11 · Fake harness, test tracker, local bare remote and scenario runner; one scenario end to end (2026-09-28)
- [x] OWL-12 · CLI: `--version`, `doctor`, `config show` (2026-09-28)

**Exit gate.** CI green on the three platforms; one scenario runs end to end with the fake harness and the test tracker; `owlshift doctor` reports the maintainer's machine correctly.

## P1 tasks & exit gate

- [x] OWL-13 · Linear tracker adapter: read a ticket, post a comment, with recorded fixtures and a conformance suite (2026-09-28, [#16](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/16))
- [x] OWL-14 · Claude Code harness adapter: headless run on the user's login, model, effort, permission level, usage capture (2026-09-28, [#15](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/15))
- [x] OWL-15 · Executor: worktree, brief, spawn, process-tree stop, result validation, isolation check (2026-09-28, [#29](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/29))
- [x] OWL-16 · Gate commands from the project config before delivery (2026-09-29, [#32](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/32))
- [x] OWL-17 · GitHub forge adapter: push the branch, open the PR, read the complete check set (2026-09-28, [#22](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/22))
- [x] OWL-18 · Writer: delivery report on the ticket (2026-09-28, [#25](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/25))
- [x] OWL-19 · Default build role prompt (2026-09-28, [#13](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/13))
- [x] OWL-20 · CLI: `init`, `do`, `logs` (2026-09-29, [#36](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/36))
- [x] OWL-41 · Confine agent runs at the OS level: the harness and the gate in `sandbox-exec` or `bwrap`, a second Claude login for agent runs (2026-09-29, [#37](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/37); the second login became a `claude setup-token` token in OWL-94)

**Exit gate.** One real Owlshift ticket delivered as a PR by `owlshift do` on the maintainer's subscription, and one run that cannot finish stopped without delivering, with no guardrail breach.

Relaxed on 2026-10-01 (OWL-113) from three delivered tickets. The maintainer stopped after the two runs below: P1 has no questions on the ticket, so a run that needs a decision can only stop and be started again by hand, and further runs wait for P2.

**Runs, 2026-10-01**, on the maintainer's machine (macOS 26.6.2, Claude Code 2.1.284, `claude-sonnet-5-5`), from `main` at `c0c0867`:

- OWL-107 (README status): stopped `blocked` after 59 s (0.18 USD). Its change was committed in the worktree, but the gate could not pass in the sandbox: four tests ran `ps`, which a Seatbelt-confined process may not exec (setuid, recorded under OWL-110). Nothing was pushed, no PR was opened. The right stop, for a reason outside the ticket.
- OWL-110 (tests without `ps`): `done`, gate passed in the sandbox, delivered as [#89](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/89) with its delivery report on the ticket, after 6 min (0.61 USD). The ubuntu leg of CI then failed: clippy refused an unused import in a module compiled on Linux only, which the gate, run on macOS, never built. Fixed by hand on the branch (`bfcc336`); CI green on the three platforms.
- No guardrail breach: the agents committed locally only; the runner pushed, opened the PR and wrote to the tracker; nothing was merged.

What the runs showed, each with its ticket:

- A confined process cannot exec a setuid binary on macOS, whatever the profile allows: the project's own tests must not rely on one (OWL-110).
- The gate runs on the host's platform only, so a change behind `cfg(target_os)` can pass it and fail CI elsewhere; `do` ends with the checks still running and nothing follows a red one. Roadmap P3 already plans a fix run on a red check (OWL-112).
- The macOS Keychain asks twice per secret for an unsigned binary rebuilt by `cargo`, until "Always Allow" is chosen; it asks again after each rebuild.

- [x] **Exit gate.** Three tickets through at least two question rounds, one of them with an incomplete answer correctly re-asked. Passed on 2026-10-07 (OWL-142): OWL-175, OWL-178 and OWL-179 below.

A round is what the term means everywhere in this design: one ask by a Build run, posted as `[owlshift] QUESTIONS · round N`. A re-ask and a reply to a counter-question stay inside their round (scenario S10: "Re-asks for an incomplete answer do not count as question rounds"). The test tickets listed their open points and asked for them to be decided on the ticket; the maintainer staged round 2: their answer to round 1 raised one new point and asked to be asked about it, which opened it. The re-ask and the counter-questions were not staged beyond the incomplete answer the ticket called for.

**Runs, 2026-10-06 and 2026-10-07**, on the maintainer's machine (macOS 26.6.2, Claude Code 2.1.284, `claude-sonnet-5-5`), with `owlshift` built from `main` at `4f8e529`, every run based on that commit. No Linear app was stored, so Owlshift wrote through the maintainer's API key and the desktop notification was the only signal. Times and costs are the sum over each ticket's agent runs (answer checks included).

- OWL-175 (CHANGELOG, the incomplete answer): round 1 asked three `scope` questions. The decider answered Q1 alone; the answer check classed Q2 and Q3 `unanswered` and the RE-ASK named those two only, noting that the closing `go` covered Q1 alone. The next answer settled them and raised a new point; the resumed Build asked it as `Q4`, which the runner refused ("question Q4 is out of order: expected Q1"), twice, and the ticket was parked (OWL-180). Restarted with `continue`, the Build asked it as round 2's `Q1`. Answered, delivered as [#143](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/143). 8 runs, 8.4 min, 0.78 USD.
- OWL-178 (SECURITY.md): round 1 asked Q1 and Q2 as `security`, Q3 as `legal`. The decider asked back on Q3; the answer check classed it `counter_question` and the REPLY proposed a wording and asked Q3 again. Round 2 asked the decider's new point, answered, delivered as [#144](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/144). 6 runs, 7.9 min, 0.55 USD.
- OWL-179 (README privacy note): round 1 asked two `scope` questions and a `legal` one, answered in full with a new point; round 2 asked it, answered, delivered as [#145](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/145). 5 runs, 7.9 min, 0.51 USD.
- OWL-173 (`owlshift hello`), not counted: round 1's Q2 drew a counter-question and a REPLY, then the answer; delivered as [#142](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/142) after one round. 4 runs, 11.3 min, 0.46 USD.
- OWL-174 (example configuration), not counted: the Build run asked nothing. It decided the ticket's three open points itself and listed them in the delivery report under "Decisions taken without a human"; delivered as [#141](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/141). Its first gate failed on a timing bound (OWL-177) and the retry passed. 2 runs, 8.7 min, 0.47 USD.
- No guardrail breach: the agents committed locally only; the runner pushed, opened the five pull requests and wrote every comment and stage move; nothing was merged. No run recorded a warning event. The five pull requests are test deliveries, closed unmerged once recorded.

What the runs showed, each with its ticket:

- A ticket with no assignee and no zone is not run: `do` names the missing decider before anything starts. The test tickets were assigned to the maintainer.
- The Build run may decide a ticket's explicit open points itself, matters of taste and scope included, so they reach neither the decider nor the resolver, and the floor never applies to them (OWL-176). The same wording on the other four tickets produced questions.
- Round 2's numbering: after a round with a re-ask, a Build run continued the numbering (`Q4`), and its retry, not told why the first result was refused, did the same, parking the ticket (OWL-180).
- `drive_does_not_wait_for_a_process_holding_the_output_open` failed once under the gate's load, 4.58 s against `2 * EXIT_GRACE` (OWL-177).
- The desktop notification shows on macOS, which the operator could not confirm for OWL-140 on 2026-10-03: shown as Script Editor's, with the ticket and its link; a click opens Script Editor. Owlshift's icon and a click that opens the ticket wait for the P11 menu-bar app (OWL-181). The Linux side is still OWL-150.
- An answer check takes 7 to 12 s and about 0.05 USD; a round's Build run that only asks, 16 to 27 s.

The smaller P3 tickets (reports owed across a crash, comment limits, recorded fixtures, CI time) are in the Linear project "P3 · Watch mode".

- [x] **Exit gate.** Three Owlshift tickets run under `owlshift watch`, from `do` to a merged pull request, with every reply continued by watch, one pull request repaired after a red check and one after a conflict, and no `continue` typed. Passed on 2026-10-13 (OWL-212): OWL-256, OWL-257 and OWL-258 below.

The test tickets were written for the gate. OWL-256 staged the red check: it asked for intra-doc links from public docs to a private function, which the project's gate (fmt, clippy, tests) passes and CI's `cargo doc` with `-D warnings` refuses. OWL-257 and OWL-258 each left one open point to the decider, and added a README section at the same place, so that merging the first made the second conflict. Nothing else was staged.

**Runs, 2026-10-13**, on the maintainer's machine (macOS 26.6.2, Claude Code 2.1.284, `claude-sonnet-5-5`, the account's default since `[models]` names none, on the fast tier too), with `owlshift` built from `main` at `34ae628`, every run based on that commit but the rebase run, based on `7268d34`. The Linear app was stored before the runs, so Owlshift wrote as the app user `owlshiftbot`. One `owlshift watch` ran throughout; the three `do` were typed in another terminal, one after the other, since the project lock lets one command work the project at a time. Times and costs are the sum over each ticket's agent runs (answer checks included); times are `run_ended`'s `elapsed_ms`, which leaves out the time the machine sleeps (OWL-262).

- OWL-256 (doc links, the red check): the Build run delivered [#218](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/218), whose `check` legs failed on `cargo doc`. Watch read the red verdict and started a fix run, which asked the decider how to make the links pass (public function, lint allowed, or links dropped): a fix run's question, with Watch as its return stage. The answer was continued by watch; the next fix run allowed `rustdoc::private_intra_doc_links` on the two functions and pushed, fix pass 1, and the checks went green. 4 runs, 25.6 min (40.7 min by the clock, the machine having slept), 0.53 USD.
- OWL-257 (README Requirements): round 1 asked which minimum versions to state; the answer was continued by watch, and the Build run delivered [#219](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/219). Its summary noted that runtime-and-operations.md already sets git 2.39.0, which the answer had missed (OWL-261). 3 runs, 10.7 min, 0.40 USD.
- OWL-258 (README Quick start, the conflict): round 1 asked whether to show `watch` after `do`; continued by watch, delivered as [#220](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/220). Once [#219](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/219) was merged, GitHub first answered its mergeability unknown, and watch waited a pass; then conflicting on `README.md`, which watch classed trivial and gave a rebase run on the fast tier. It kept both sections and force-pushed with a lease, repair 1; the checks went green. 4 runs, 25.3 min, 0.42 USD.
- Watch ended the following of each pull request once it read it merged. The maintainer merged the three after their checks passed.
- No `continue` was typed. The first `do OWL-256` was stopped twice with Ctrl-C, to start the tickets in parallel; each stop left the marker of a run whose isolation check had not passed, and `do` and watch refused to work the project until a person looked. The dedicated checkout was found clean, the marker deleted, and `do` typed again. These are restarts of a `do`, not continues.
- No guardrail breach: the agents committed locally only; the runner pushed, opened the three pull requests and wrote every comment and stage move; nothing was merged by Owlshift. No run recorded a warning event.

What the runs showed, each with its ticket:

- A repair run works under a stage that says In Review: OWL-256's fix run changed the pull request for minutes while Linear showed it in review (OWL-259).
- The project's gate does not run rustdoc with `-D warnings`, which CI does, so a delivery can pass it and go red (OWL-260).
- `elapsed_ms` is a monotonic time, which does not count a sleep on macOS (OWL-262). Since OWL-262, `run_ended` also carries `wall_ms`.
- `do` exits with a failure when it stops on questions, so `owlshift do A && owlshift do B` stops at the first ticket that asks. Not a fault: it stopped without delivering.
- While waiting for its own `cargo test`, a Build run looped on `pgrep -f "cargo "`, which also waits for any other cargo process of the machine.
- A run's Build takes 5 to 15 minutes, mostly the gate's tests; an answer check, 7 to 9 s and about 0.05 USD; a run that only asks, 19 to 30 s.

## P4 · The drain

Designed on 2026-10-13 (OWL-244). The rules are [architecture](architecture.md) section 5, "The drain": admission, picking, intake, back-pressure and follow-ups. Below, what was checked live for them, the contracts they change, who builds what, so that no implementation ticket decides them again, and how the exit gate is run. The implementation tickets live in the Linear project "P4 · The drain", one per part below, filed on 2026-10-13 with the `blocks` relations listed in pull request #215.

**Checked live on 2026-10-13**, read-only, on the Owlshift workspace through the personal API key (GraphQL, `organization { urlKey }` answering `owlshift` first), and on `pitchopp/owlshift` through the maintainer's `gh` login. A one-off script, so no fixture was recorded; the adapter work records its own, as OWL-13 did.

- *Priority.* By introspection, `Issue.priority` is "0 = No priority, 1 = Urgent, 2 = High, 3 = Medium, 4 = Low", as C4 found; OWL-82 and OWL-156 answer 0 with `priorityLabel` "No priority". `issues(sort: [{ priority: { order: Descending } }])` over the open tickets gave the one high ticket, then the medium ones, then the low ones, each group roughly newest first (OWL-243, OWL-212, OWL-181 …); `Ascending` gave the low ones first. `prioritySortOrder` ("the order … when ordered by priority") and `sortOrder` (manual order) exist, and neither is the order seen. So the runner sorts by itself (architecture, "Order").
- *Relations.* `IssueRelationType` has `blocks`, `duplicate`, `related` and `similar`; `IssueRelation` has `type`, `issue` and `relatedIssue`. The direction, read on OWL-124, OWL-118, OWL-99, OWL-142 and OWL-223, whose descriptions name their blockers: each holds a `blocks` entry in `inverseRelations` whose `issue` is the blocker, so `issue` blocks `relatedIssue`, and every blocker named in their text had its relation.
- *States.* Team `OWL` has Triage on (`triageEnabled`) and nine states, by type: Triage `triage`; Backlog `backlog`; Todo `unstarted`; In Progress, Needs Input and In Review `started`; Done `completed`; Canceled `canceled`; Duplicate `duplicate`. The schema's `WorkflowState.type` lists those seven types. `owlshift.toml` maps ready to Todo, so the ready column is the team's one `unstarted` state, and no ticket was in it that day.
- *The listing.* `issues(first: 50, filter: { team: { key: { eq: "OWL" } }, state: { name: { eq: <ready> } }, labels: { name: { eq: <label> } } })` with the fields the drain reads (identifier, title, description, priority, creation time, assignee, `labels(first: 50)`, `inverseRelations(first: 50) { type issue { identifier state { type } } }`, `pageInfo`), run on the Canceled state and the label `agent` since Todo was empty, answered the five P2 exit gate tickets (OWL-173 to OWL-179), their labels and assignee, with `x-complexity: 171`. The label `agent` exists on team `OWL`. Checked again that day for part B (OWL-246): `inverseRelations` leaves out an archived blocker unless asked with `includeArchived: true` (OWL-116 listed none without it, its archived blockers OWL-114 and OWL-23 with it), so the adapter asks for them, since the team archives a closed issue after a month and a canceled blocker would otherwise vanish and release the ticket.
- *The gesture's account.* OWL-129's history holds the entry that added `zone:backend/billing`, with `addedLabels` and its `actor`, the maintainer's account (`app` false). OWL-179, created with `agent`, has no history entry for it, only its state moves. So a label set at creation is read from the creator. `IssueHistory` also has `toState` and `botActor`, which a state gesture and an app's own move are read from.
- *Delegation.* `Issue.delegate`, "the agent user that is delegated to work on this issue", exists. The app of D8 took no `app:assignable` scope (OWL-141), so no issue can be delegated to it.
- *The merge and Done.* OWL-242's history moves it to a `completed` state at 08:39 UTC with `botActor` "GitHub" (type `integration`), at the merge of [#213](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/213), which is attached to it; the workspace's integrations list `github`, `githubCommit` and `githubCodeAccessPersonal`. So the Linear GitHub integration is connected now, which AGENTS.md still describes as pending.
- *Finding a proposal again.* `description: { contains: … }` found OWL-156, canceled, without `includeArchived`; `title: { eqIgnoreCase: "design the drain (p4) in the build plan" }` found OWL-244; OWL-167, deleted to the trash, was not found. `searchIssues(term: …)` is a ranked search that also answered completed tickets on related words, so dedup uses the two exact filters.
- *The text convention.* `description: { containsIgnoreCase: "blocked by" }` found twelve tickets: OWL-218 to OWL-223, OWL-124 and OWL-118 write "Blocked by OWL-n." or "Blocked by OWL-n and OWL-m." on a line of its own, OWL-221 adds "(same line)", OWL-116 "and by the verdict-folding follow-up", OWL-142 "Blocked by the remaining P2 work", OWL-99 a heading "## Blocked by" with the identifier on the next line, and OWL-162 "as follow-ups blocked by this issue" mid-sentence.
- *Rate limits.* Every answer carries `x-ratelimit-requests-limit: 2500`, `x-ratelimit-complexity-limit: 3000000` and the query's own `x-complexity`: 7 for `organization`, 139 for the open tickets with their relations, 171 for the listing above. The requests left read 2,499 on each answer.
- *The free plan.* 244 issues made, 234 not archived, of which 217 completed or canceled; the team archives a closed issue after one month (`autoArchivePeriod` 1) and closes a stale one after six (`autoClosePeriod` 6). Linear's free plan holds 250 active issues (AGENTS.md).
- *GitHub.* `gh api rate_limit`: 5,000 an hour for `core` and for `graphql` on the maintainer's token. `gh pr list --state open`: [#214](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/214) alone, its `reviewDecision` empty, since no review is required on this private repository on a free plan (OWL-17 found its branch unprotected).
- *Pending,* each the first step of its part below, since a write is not a read-only check: (d) `issueRelationCreate(input: { type: blocks, issueId, relatedIssueId })`, which the schema has, writes the relation the read above shows, with identifiers in place of ids: run on 2026-10-14 (OWL-249) through the personal API key on the Owlshift workspace, `organization { urlKey }` answering `owlshift` first, on two throwaway issues made in Backlog for it, OWL-271 and OWL-272, both canceled at the end, never deleted, every answer HTTP 200. `issueRelationCreate(input: { type: blocks, issueId: "OWL-271", relatedIssueId: "OWL-272" })` answered `success: true` and the relation, `issue` OWL-271, `relatedIssue` OWL-272; read back, OWL-272 listed it in its `inverseRelations` (its state of type `backlog`) and OWL-271 in its `relations`. The same write again answered `success: true` with the same relation's id, and OWL-272 still listed one `blocks` relation: a write of a relation already there is idempotent. The reverse write, OWL-272 blocking OWL-271, answered `success: true` and a new relation: Linear does not refuse a cycle, so the runner's walk is the only guard. Read back on 2026-10-15 (OWL-292, part I), such a reverse write replaces the first relation, so this check did not show Linear holding a cycle: a pair holds one relation, and a `blocks` write replaces its `related` or reverse `blocks` relation without a word. The runner's walk refuses the reverse write before it is made; a cycle through three tickets or more was not checked. `issueId: "OWL-99999"` answered `data` null and one error `issue not found`, code `INPUT_ERROR` (type `invalid input`, `userPresentableMessage` "Issue 'OWL-99999' not found."), where a read answers `Entity not found: Issue`; (e) `issueCreate` into Triage with `projectId`, then a `related` relation to the parent, through the app user, then canceled, never deleted (C4's way): run on 2026-10-13, below.
- *Check (e), run on 2026-10-13* (OWL-252) on the Owlshift workspace, `organization { urlKey }` answering `owlshift` first, by a one-off script, so no fixture was recorded. Through a client-credentials token of the app (scopes `read,write`, `viewer.app` true, revoked at the end): `issueCreate(input: { teamId, stateId, projectId, title, description })` with OWL's Triage state and OWL-252's project answered `success: true` and OWL-266 in Triage (type `triage`) in "P4 · The drain", its creator the app user, no label; `issueRelationCreate(input: { type: related, issueId: "OWL-266", relatedIssueId: "OWL-252" })` took both identifiers and answered the relation; read back 3 s later, OWL-266 was still in Triage, `triagedAt` null, the relation in its `relations`. `IssueCreateInput` has `projectId` and `labelIds` but no relation field, so the relations are writes of their own after the creation. Through the API key, `issues(filter: { team: { key: { eq: "OWL" } }, title: { eqIgnoreCase: <the title lower-cased> } }, includeArchived: true)` and the same with `description: { contains: "fingerprint <hex>" }` each answered OWL-266 alone, and the latter again once it was canceled. The script then deleted the relation and moved OWL-266 to Canceled. A second script that day, through the app again: `issueRelationCreate(input: { type: blocks, issueId: <OWL-252's id>, relatedIssueId: <OWL-266's id> })` answered the relation, which OWL-266 then listed in its `inverseRelations` with OWL-252 as `issue`, and was deleted; through the key, `issueLabels(filter: { name: { eq: "agent" } })` answered team OWL's label with its team, and `eq: "Agent"` nothing. So a proposal is filed into Triage with its project and relations, and both exact searches find it, as section 5 "Follow-ups" has it. The two relations were written by separate scripts, never on one pair at once; on one pair, Linear keeps the later only (OWL-292, part I).

**Contracts and formats.**

- *`owlshift.toml`.* `[tracker].admitters`, a list of tracker account identifiers, optional for the file and required by `--drain`; `[tracker].exclude`, an optional label; `policy.max_open_prs`, from 1 to 20, 3 when absent. `admit = { state }` is refused unless it names `states.ready`. A file using a new key raises its `requires` to the first Owlshift that reads it, as zones did. As built (OWL-245): `admitters`, when present, lists at least one account, each once, written exactly as zone owners are, none blank, padded or holding a control character; `exclude` is such a name too, and is refused when it is the admission label in any case, which would exclude every ticket.
- *The ticket ref.* `intake.json` (contract `TicketIntake`, format 1, with its schema): the text's hash, the gesture (label or state, the account, the tracker's time), the routing verdict (variant, harness, tier, effort), and each dependency with its quote and outcome (`written`, `existed`, `done`, `unknown`, `itself` or `cycle`, or `pending` while part F has not checked it: intake records each dependency `pending`, added by OWL-248 within format 1, since no record had been written). `proposals.json` (contract `TicketProposals`, format 1): each proposal with its fingerprint, title, the run that proposed it, and its outcome (`owed` with the last refusal, `filed` with the ticket, `duplicate` with the ticket, or `over_limit`). As built (OWL-245): the proposal is kept whole (`proposal`, the run's `Followup`: title, why, evidence, source, definition of done, and whether it waits for the parent), since a filing the tracker refused stays owed and a later pass files it; the fingerprint is its 12 hexadecimal digits, as the footer shows them; the run is its kind (`delivery`, `fix`, `rebase` or `correction`) and count, as `delivery.json` counts pushes. An intake record names at most 64 dependencies, each quote not blank, on one line and at most 512 bytes, so the record stays far below the ticket ref's 1 MiB per file. Both are read when present and written only when they have content; `owlshift forget` drops the intake record, so the next pass runs intake again, and keeps `proposals.json`, so nothing is filed twice. A ref that intake makes holds `state.json` and a `questions.json` with no ask, one more exception to "makes no ticket ref until a round opens".
- *`result.json` format 8.* `intake`: `variant`, `routing` (`harness`, `tier`, `effort`) and `dependencies` (`ticket`, `quote`). Required from the intake role with `done` or `questions`, refused from any other role. As built (OWL-245): refused with any other status too, and `effort` is one of `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` and `max`, the levels `claude --help` listed for `--effort` on 2026-10-13 (Claude Code 2.1.296); a harness with other levels maps from them in P5. The brief needs no new field: the intake role reads the ticket, its thread, its decider and the rules.
- *Marked comments.* `[owlshift] INTAKE` on the ticket; the `[owlshift] PROPOSAL · <parent> · fingerprint <hex>` footer in a proposal's description. The DELIVERY report's line for a follow-up names its outcome: filed as OWL-n in Triage, already proposed as OWL-n, not filed past the three per parent, or owed with why.
- *Events.* `decision` events with `drain` = `waits` (and the reason), `pr_cap`, `intake` or `proposal`; `warning` events `not_admitted` and `proposal_unfiled`. Free-form data, so the event format stays 2.
- *The core.* The text convention, the order, the release of a blocker, the slot and the ready set's reasons (no decider, no intake record or a stale one, a blocker canceled, a blocker unknown, the cap, the slot) are pure functions over plain data; the ready set ignores a ticket's own identifier among its blockers (OWL-264). Hashing stays out of the core, which depends on `serde` and `schemars` only: the runner computes the text's hash and the fingerprint, the core normalizes the title.

**Who builds what.**

- **A · Configuration and ticket-ref contracts.** (OWL-245) The keys, the `admit.state` rule, `TicketIntake`, `TicketProposals` and their schemas, `result.json` format 8.
- **B · Reading the ready column.** (OWL-246) The tracker trait's listing of admitted tickets (the `ListAdmitted` capability), with their blockers and their state types, and the gesture's account from the history; on Linear the query above, paged; on the Markdown tracker its folders, `blocked_by`, a blocker done at the stage `Done`, and the ticket's `author` as the gesture's account, since its files keep no history. Recorded fixtures.
- **C · The ready set.** (OWL-247) In `owlshift-core`: the text convention, the order, the release rule, the one slot and the reasons, extending `schedule::ready_set` and `Ticket`. `TicketState::restore` accepts a ticket at Intake with rounds asked before it was taken in again, with no re-ask and not waiting.
- **D · Doctor.** (OWL-155) `admit.state` checked as a tracker state and against `states.ready`; the admission label and the exclusion label exist on the team or the workspace; `admitters` set, each an account of the workspace; `"delegation"` a warning; the team's triage inbox, or the label fallback. Each a warning, since none blocks `do`, with the note that `--drain` refuses to start. As built (OWL-155), checked live on 2026-10-14 (read only, personal API key, `organization { urlKey }` answering `owlshift` first; no fixture recorded, the unit tests answer through a scripted transport): `teams(filter: { key })` answers `triageEnabled` and each state's `type` (OWL: true, Triage `triage`); `issueLabels(first: 50, filter: { name: { in: [...] } })` is exact, case included (`agent` and `Agent` asked, only `agent` answered, with its `team` and `isGroup` false; `agent:proposed` and an unknown name nothing), a workspace label having `team` null (not observed: this workspace has none); `users(first: 50, includeDisabled: true, filter: { id: { in: [...] } })` answers `id`, `active` and `app` for a real id, leaves an unknown one out, and costs 65 to 70 complexity points, where `user(id:)` with an unknown id fails with an input error (`Entity not found: User`), so the check never asks `user(id:)`; the unfiltered `users` listing hides a deactivated account, and `includeDisabled` was accepted but there is no deactivated account here to show it listed (the field `active` is the schema's). The line `drain admission` warns of: `"delegation"`; `admitters` unset; an admission or exclusion label found on neither the team nor the workspace (a label group counts as absent, since no ticket can carry one); an admitter that is no account of the workspace, an app user (which never admits) or a deactivated account. Delegation and an unset `admitters` need no tracker and are said for a Markdown project too, which has no other admission line. It reads `admitters` from the working tree, where the drain reads the base commit, and says so. The line `proposals inbox` uses the choice `propose` makes (`proposals_inbox`, shared): the triage inbox when the team has triage on and a `triage` state, else its first `backlog` state with the label `agent:proposed` on the team or the workspace; it warns when that label is missing, when there is no backlog state, or when the state chosen is the ready state. Linear unreachable gives one `labels and admitters not checked` note on the admission line and nothing on the inbox line; a missing key or an unreadable keychain adds nothing, since the tracker states line fails on it. More than 50 admitters or label matches are refused, not truncated.
- **E · Intake.** (OWL-248) `roles/intake.md`; the run, read-only without network on the standard tier; the record and the INTAKE comment; its questions through the resolver to a round returning to Ready, whose RESUME moves the ticket back to the ready state; `continue` on a ticket at Intake. As built (OWL-248): `OnDemand::intake` is the entry point part G calls, with the gesture it checked; the runner asserts again that its account is in `[tracker].admitters` at the base commit before writing the record. "Without network" is the harness's tool list (Claude Code's web tools removed, every tool that would ask denied), as for the resolver and the answer check: the system sandbox leaves the network open to the harness. The text's hash is taken from the ticket as the brief holds it, never read again after the run. A dependency is kept only when its identifier is in its quote and in the ticket's title, description or a comment of its thread, and a result naming another is refused; a refused result, and a `premise_false`, which the result contract already refuses from intake without a record, is a failed run, the next run not told why, since the brief gives a refusal only to Build, the answer check and the resolver. The INTAKE comment shows each quote flattened in a code span, and each dependency "not checked yet" until part F; a `gate` event `intake` carries the variant, the routing and the dependency count. The INTAKE comment is posted before the record is kept; a record not kept after it stops the command as a comment not kept does. An intake that ends at Ready moves the ticket back to the ready state while the tracker shows needs input or parked (architecture, "Back to the ready column"), and `continue` on a ticket at Ready with an intake record makes that move again, then stops: the ticket waits for the drain. A ticket parked at Intake restarted by `continue` keeps its parked stage while intake runs. `continue` at Intake takes the kept gesture when its account is still listed and the record holds the current text, else reads the gesture again, which must be a listed account: a parked intake on a project that no longer lists its admitter is refused. On the Markdown tracker, which keeps no history, the gesture's time is when it was read. Since OWL-269 (architecture, "Its questions"), an intake round returns to Intake in the core, and its RESUME lands there: `continue`, typed or watch's, runs intake again in the same command, the ticket kept at needs input until the new record is kept, so a dependency the answer names is recorded and related. A ref whose round was asked before it still returns to Ready.
- **F · Dependencies to relations.** (OWL-249) Pending check (d) first. The Writer's relation write on Linear and on the Markdown tracker (`blocked_by`), and the checks before it, the cycle up to ten deep included. As built (OWL-249): check (d) ran first (above). Two `Tracker` methods, each an error by default: `relations(id)`, the tracker's own identifier, its state's type, its description and its blockers as the listing reads them (on Linear one `issue(id:)` read through the key, whose `inverseRelations` selection the listing shares; on the Markdown tracker only a ticket file that is not there is not found), and `block(blocker, blocked)`, Linear's `issueRelationCreate` by identifiers through the app, the Markdown tracker's `blocked_by` line rewritten or added and nothing else, a key written over several lines refused. The decision is `owlshift_core::dependency::check`, in the order itself, unknown, existed (a relation of the ticket already names it), done, cycle, then write, on the tracker's identifiers; the walk follows each ticket's relations and its "Blocked by" line, breadth first, reading each ticket once and the tenth depth not at all. The runner checks a verdict when it is kept, with its round or at its end, so a verdict the resolver sends back to a new run writes nothing; a dependency named twice is checked once. Each relation written is a `tracker_write` event (`action` `relation`, `type` `blocks`, `from`, `to`) under the floor's `Relate` action; a failed read or write is a failed run with a `relations_not_written` warning (architecture, "Dependencies to relations"). Each ticket is read at most once per verdict, so a dense graph within ten levels costs one read per ticket reached.
- **G · The drain in watch.** (OWL-250) `--drain`; the listing each pass; the gesture's account checked and kept; one intake then at most one dispatch per pass, through `do`'s path under the lock; the reasons' events and lines. The text convention's identifiers that are not among the ticket's relations are read on the tracker each pass, each passed to the core with the state read (architecture, "Blockers", OWL-264): a definite "not found" is `BlockerState::Unknown`, any other failed read is `Open`, so it holds the ticket under `BlockedBy` and is read again on the next pass; the ticket's own identifier is passed as is, the core ignores it. On the Markdown tracker, a line's identifier with no ticket folder is unknown; a front-matter `blocked_by` with none is listed without a state by part B and waits as open, named. Checked on 2026-10-14 (OWL-264) with the personal API key on the Owlshift workspace: `issue(id: "OWL-99999")`, `issue(id: "UTF-8")` and `issue(id: "ENG-1")` (a key no team has) each answered HTTP 200, `data.issue` null and one error `Entity not found: Issue` (code `INPUT_ERROR`, type `invalid input`); `issue(id: "OWL-264")` answered the issue and its state type `started`. As built (OWL-250): `--drain` refuses to start on `admit = "delegation"` or with no `admitters` in the working tree's project file. After watch's own work, each pass reads the ticket refs again, so a merge watch read in that pass releases its dependents, then lists the column; a listing that fails is one line and the drain does nothing more that pass. The text convention's identifiers are read through `Tracker::state_type` (a default method: on Linear `issue(id:) { state { type } }`, on the Markdown tracker `Done` or no folder), only for tickets whose intake record holds their current text. Intake and dispatch (`OnDemand::intake_seen`, `OnDemand::dispatch_seen`) take the lock, then check that the ref is the one read, that the ticket still shows the ready state and the admission label without the exclusion label, and the gesture against `admitters` at the base commit by one rule shared with `continue` at Intake: the kept gesture while the record holds the current text and its account is listed, else the gesture read again. So a dropped admitter's ticket is not dispatched until its gesture, read again, admits it. The dispatch also re-reads every ticket ref for the slot. A ticket taken in during a pass is left out of that pass's ready set, so the relations its intake writes are read first. A ticket ref that cannot be read stops every dispatch, since it may hold the slot, and is said in one line. A gesture that does not admit is warned of once per ticket and account and read again after watch's ten-minute hold. Intakes and dispatches share watch's holds: a refusal that leaves the ref as it was (an absent one included) waits ten minutes, and a usage limit pauses both. The `decision` events are `drain` = `intake` and `drain` = `waits` (with `reason`, the core's `Wait::key`, and `tickets` for blockers); the `warning` is `not_admitted` with the account. Accepted residuals: a ticket whose zones name no owner counts as having a decider until its intake is refused, which watch then prints each ten minutes; the warnings already given are forgotten when watch restarts. The cap is part H's. As built (OWL-291): `Wait::Slot` names its holders, the `Load`'s `slot_held_by` (each ref read whose state holds the slot or does not read, and each ref that cannot be read) and the column's own, in identifier order, or the ticket dispatched in the pass, in `tickets` and on the line; doctor's `drain slot` line reads the refs through `ticket_ref::states`, with no event format change.
- **H · The open pull request cap.** (OWL-251) The count from the ticket refs, the hold on dispatch, its event and line. It waits for OWL-243, so that a parked ticket's merged pull request stops counting. As built (OWL-251): each pass counts the ticket refs it read whose delivery is followed (a pull request, not ended), whatever the ticket's state, and passes it with `policy.max_open_prs()` to the core's `Caps` and `Load`; a ref that cannot be read is not counted, since it already stops every dispatch. A ticket the cap holds back is not told one by one: the `waits` event and line skip `pr_cap`. Instead one `decision` event (`drain` = `pr_cap`, with `open_prs` and `max_open_prs`) and one line are emitted when a ready ticket is first held by the cap, on the first ticket held (an event names a ticket), and again only after the count has fallen below the cap and reached it anew. A cap reached while no ready ticket waits is told when one does; the ticket named is only the event's carrier. A pass with an unread ref neither re-arms nor tells the cap, its count being short. Intake goes on at the cap. As built (OWL-313): the check also holds under the lock. `OnDemand::dispatch_seen` counts the followed deliveries again from every ticket ref it reads for the slot under the lock, its own included, and starts no Build at the cap. So a pull request delivered by a `do` or a `continue` typed since the snapshot holds the dispatch (`a_delivery_since_the_snapshot_holds_the_dispatch`). The held dispatch tells the cap as the pass would, through the same event, line and re-arming, with the dispatched ticket as carrier. Nothing ran, so watch's ten-minute hold does not apply, and the next pass counts again.
- **I · Proposals in the tracker.** (OWL-252) Pending check (e) first. The adapter's proposal: into the triage state, or the backlog with `agent:proposed`; its relations and project; the two exact searches. As built (OWL-252), on Linear only: `Tracker::propose` takes the title and the description the runner rendered, footer included, whether the parent blocks it, and `states.ready`. It files into the team's first `triage` state by position when the team has triage on, else into its first `backlog` state with the `agent:proposed` label, the team's before the workspace's, never created; it refuses, before any write, a target state named as the ready state, the floor's check made where the target is known. The ticket in place, a relation not written or another state answered is kept in `Filed::problems` and the filing succeeds, since the fingerprint search would count it filed anyway: J warns of them. `Tracker::proposed(team, title, marker)` runs the two filters with `includeArchived`, leaves completed tickets out and answers each ticket once with its description; the runner renders the marker, the footer's `fingerprint <hex>`, and reads the parent from the description. The `blocks` write (parent as `issueId`) was checked with (e). The Markdown tracker's proposal is part M's (OWL-267); the `ProposalsInbox` capability is declared with J, whose doctor line names it. Checked live on 2026-10-15 (OWL-292), after the P4 exit gate's proposals OWL-286 and OWL-287 were found holding only the `blocks` relation from OWL-281: through the personal API key on the Owlshift workspace, `organization { urlKey }` answering `owlshift` first, by a one-off script, on two throwaway issues made in Backlog, OWL-294 standing for a parent and OWL-295 for its proposal, both canceled at the end, never deleted, every answer HTTP 200. The relations were written by the issues' ids, as the adapter writes them, read back 5 s later on both issues (`relations` and `inverseRelations` with `includeArchived: true`), then each by its id, and deleted before the next order. `related` from OWL-295 to OWL-294 then `blocks` from OWL-294 to OWL-295, the adapter's order, each answered `success: true` and a relation of its own; read back, the pair held the `blocks` relation alone, and the `related` one's id answered `Entity not found: IssueRelation`. `blocks` then `related` left `related` alone, and `blocks` one way then the other left the later alone, the earlier one's id not found each time. OWL-286's history shows the app adding both its relations to OWL-281 at 00:15:12 on the 15th, and its decline at 00:16 changing none. So Linear keeps one relation between two issues, the later write replacing the earlier without a word, in the three orders tried; `similar`, `duplicate` and the app's own writes on throwaway issues were not tried. As built (OWL-292): `propose` writes one relation, `blocks` with the parent as `issueId` when the parent blocks the proposal, else `related` with the proposal as `issueId`; a filing whose one relation and state are as asked has no problem, so a `blocks`-only proposal is never reported as missing its `related` relation.
- **J · Filing follow-ups.** (OWL-253) `proposals.json` owed with the delivery or the repair, filed before the DELIVERY report, kept as filed; the duplicates and the three per parent; the report's lines; the floor's refusal of a proposal in the ready state or with the admission label. As built (OWL-253), the rules of architecture section 5, "Follow-ups", with its "As built" notes: `proposals.rs` in the runner owes (`owe`), searches and files (`file_owed`, through `Writer::propose`, which asks `floor::check_proposal`), and the core normalizes the title (`proposal::normalize_title`). `proposals.json` stays out of `TicketRecord`: `ticket_ref::Stored` reads it, `ticket_ref::write` keeps the file it finds, `write_proposals` writes it, and `forget` keeps it unless it no longer parses. A report's line names the outcome as "filed as OWL-n for triage", not the state, since `ProposalOutcome::Filed` keeps none and the inbox is a backlog state on a team without triage; then "not filed, already proposed as OWL-n", "not filed past the three per ticket", "owed, not filed yet" with the last refusal, and, for a follow-up never owed, "not filed" as before. Events: `decision` with `drain` = `proposal` and the outcome, `tracker_write` with `action` = `propose`, and the warnings `proposal_unfiled` and `proposal_incomplete` (a `Filed::problems` entry). `LinearTracker` declares `ProposalsInbox`. The Markdown tracker's inbox is part M's (OWL-267), which K needs to play S13. Since OWL-268 (architecture, "A relation lost after filing"), a proposal that needs its parent's code names it on the line `**Blocked by** <parent>, whose code it needs.` of its description, in place of the sentence that said so, and the drain reads that line as a blocker once a person promotes the proposal; a relation not written after the filing stays a `proposal_incomplete` warning and is never written again, the promoted proposal's intake relating the parent through part F.
- **K · The scenarios.** (OWL-254) S3, S4, S6, S12 and S13 played by the test bench on the Markdown tracker, as acceptance; S13 through the Markdown inbox part M builds. As built (OWL-254): played in Rust on the runner's own `owlshift watch --drain` (`Watch::run_drain`), with the Markdown tracker, the fake harness and the bench's fake GitHub, one test per scenario, two for S4 (`scenario_s3_questions_at_intake`, `scenario_s4_unblocked_by_a_merge` and `scenario_s4_the_blocked_branch_starts_from_the_merge`, `scenario_s6_the_human_is_the_bottleneck`, `scenario_s12_the_human_keeps_control`, `scenario_s13_proposed_follow_ups`) in `owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand/drain_scenarios.rs`, a module of `on_demand.rs` whose bench they share, as P3's S9 is played there. Not as files of `tests/scenarios/`: their stand-in driver keeps the core state in memory and runs neither `OnDemand` nor watch, so a drain step there would play a copy of the drain. A merge is the forge's: `main` moved to the delivered branch and its pull request read merged, the ticket's stage left as it was, so only watch's read of the merge releases what it blocked. The bench's fake GitHub keeps the branch of its one open pull request, so another ticket delivers once the first is merged, and its stand-in inbox gives way to the Markdown tracker's own on request. They run on Linux and macOS; on Windows they are built and linted, not run, as every test of `on_demand.rs` since OWL-241. What each plays, by the roadmap's readings of P4: *S3*, intake once per text; an intake round at needs input, its answer continued by watch and intake run again, a prerequisite named only in the answer related; a dependency named in prose related at the first intake; intake again after an edit, before any dispatch; the routing and the dependencies on each INTAKE comment; no model run between the last intake and Build. Resources wait for P6. *S4*, the blocked ticket dispatched in the pass that reads its blocker's merge; a chain of three in one watch, one link a relation, the other a "Blocked by" line. Its branch starting from the merged `main`, which the first intake made at the base of its time and the dispatch fast-forwards (OWL-274, architecture section 5, "How the branch reaches that base"), is `scenario_s4_the_blocked_branch_starts_from_the_merge`'s. *S6*, a Build of the drain's cut by a usage limit stays at Build and holds the one slot until a person types `continue` (P4's reading; a person's own `do` cut so keeps no ticket ref, as before, and holds nothing); at `max_open_prs` intake goes on and no Build starts, told once; the merge releases the held Build in its pass; a notification line for the usage limit, none for an intake that ends done, a delivery or the cap. A configurable cap on concurrent runs waits for P6, the digest for P7. *S12*, the exclusion label keeps a ticket out before its intake and after it; a ticket without the label, from an account not listed, or moved out of the column by a person, before its intake or after it, gets no comment, stage or ticket ref from Owlshift from then on. `owlshift pause` waits for P7, a person's own work counted in flight for P6's collisions. *S13*, through the Markdown inbox: proposals at `Triage`, authored `owlshift`, `related` to the parent and `blocked_by` it when they need its code, each with its why, evidence, source and definition of done; a duplicate of an open ticket or of another parent's declined proposal not filed, a follow-up given twice filed once, three per parent, the rest named in the DELIVERY report; none taken in at `Triage` or `Backlog`, nor in the column while `owlshift` is its author; taken in once a person makes it theirs, then blocked by its parent. Played elsewhere: the labelled backlog of a tracker without triage, on Linear (parts I and J); the floor's refusal of a proposal in the ready state or with the admission label (its unit tests); the notification of a re-ask or a park (`notify`'s); the column's priority order (the core's). Not played: the build role's rule that its own acceptance criteria are questions, never follow-ups, which the runner cannot see (architecture section 5, "Follow-ups").
- **L · The exit gate** (OWL-255), below.
- **M · Proposals on the Markdown tracker.** (OWL-267) Added on 2026-10-14 for K, which plays S13 on that tracker, since I built the proposal on Linear only. As built (OWL-267): `MarkdownTracker` declares `ProposalsInbox`; `propose` files at the stage `Triage` as "The test tracker" above has it, refusing first a ready state of that name, and answers `Filed` with no problem, one file being written; `proposed` reads every ticket folder, leaves out those at `Done`, and answers those whose title matches once both are lowercased (Unicode's, as close as a file comes to Linear's `eqIgnoreCase`) or whose description holds the marker, a ticket that cannot be read failing the search. The `Tracker` trait is unchanged. Doctor's `proposals inbox` line covers a Markdown project from its file alone: the stage `Triage`, or a warning when the ready state is named so. The test bench's stand-in inbox (`tests/on_demand.rs`) now replaces the Markdown tracker's own, and when off hides the capability, so the tests of a tracker without an inbox keep their meaning.

## P4 tasks & exit gate

The tasks are the parts above, OWL-245 to OWL-255, OWL-155 and OWL-267, in the Linear project "P4 · The drain".

**Exit gate.** Ten Owlshift tickets drained serially, at least one started by the merge of its blocker. Run by the maintainer, as P2's was, once K passes and P3's exit gate (OWL-212) has:

1. *Before.* `owlshift.toml` on `main` names the maintainer's Linear account under `admitters` and keeps `admit = { label = "agent" }`; `owlshift doctor` shows no admission warning. The workspace has room for the tickets and their proposals under the free plan's 250 active issues (above): 16 on 2026-10-13, before the P4 tickets themselves are filed.
2. *The tickets.* At least ten real Owlshift tickets, each assigned to the maintainer, labelled `agent` and moved to Todo. Among them, a pair where one blocks the other, by a relation or a "Blocked by" line, both admitted while the blocker is still open. If the backlog has no such pair, one is staged, as P2 staged its round 2.
3. *The run.* `owlshift watch --drain` on the maintainer's machine, from `main` at a recorded commit, for as long as the ten take. The maintainer answers on the tickets, reviews and merges the pull requests, and triages the proposals; they type nothing for a drained ticket but the `continue`s watch leaves to a person by design, each recorded with its reason, as P3's gate does.
4. *What counts.* A ticket is drained when its `dispatch` event names `command` = `drain` and the drain carried it, through its intake and any round, to a delivered pull request. Serially: in the event log, no two tickets are ever between their dispatch and their delivery at once. Started by a merge: the blocked ticket's `dispatch` follows its blocker's merge, read by watch (`ended`, `merged`) or shown by Linear's Done, in the first pass that finds its slot free.
5. *Recorded here,* as P2's runs are: per ticket, its runs, minutes (the sum of `run_ended`'s `wall_ms`, which counts a sleep; OWL-262) and cost; the intake rounds; the dependencies written; each proposal and its outcome; the cap, if reached; and that there was no guardrail breach (the agents committed locally only, the runner wrote every comment, relation and proposal, nothing was merged by Owlshift).

- [x] **Exit gate.** Passed on 2026-10-15 (OWL-255): OWL-276 to OWL-285 below, ten tickets drained serially, OWL-285 started by the merge of its blocker OWL-276.

The test tickets were written for the gate, as P2's and P3's were: the backlog held no ten tickets of the current step a Build could finish, so ten small changes were filed with a title starting `[P4 gate]`, each assigned to the maintainer, labelled `agent` by them and moved to Todo. All but OWL-276 add throwaway work to `gate/p4/` or `owlshift-testkit` and were closed unmerged once recorded; OWL-276 only adds `gate/p4/README.md`, merged so that OWL-285, whose description reads "Blocked by OWL-276.", is started by its merge; this pull request deletes the folder. Staged on purpose: the blocking pair; an open point the decider must settle on OWL-277 and OWL-280; a prerequisite named in prose without an identifier on OWL-278, which intake must ask about; two out-of-scope changes OWL-281 must report as follow-ups. OWL-285's question was not staged: it asked for the ten titles, which an agent without network cannot read.

**Runs, 2026-10-14 and 15**, on the maintainer's machine (macOS 26.6.2, Claude Code 2.1.300, `claude-sonnet-5-5` on every run, `[models]` naming none), one `owlshift watch --drain` from 22:00 UTC to the end, every Build based on `main` at `7bf1d1e` but OWL-285's, based on the merge `60ae3f1`. Owlshift wrote as the Linear app user. The answers on the tickets were written by Claude on the maintainer's behalf and posted with their API key, so they count as the decider's; Claude also closed the unmerged pull requests and declined the proposals; the maintainer merged [#248](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/248). Runs, minutes and cost are the sum over each ticket's agent runs, intake and answer checks included; minutes are `run_ended`'s `wall_ms` (OWL-262).

- OWL-276 (the folder, the blocker): intake found no dependency; Build delivered [#248](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/248), merged at 06:29 on the 15th. 2 runs, 21.6 min, 0.21 USD.
- OWL-277 (glossary, an open point): intake asked which three terms (round 1); the answer was continued by watch and intake ran again; delivered [#249](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/249). 4 runs, 30.9 min, 0.37 USD.
- OWL-278 (haiku, a prerequisite in prose): intake asked which ticket "the glossary ticket" is and whether to wait for it, a `dependency` question the resolver passed on to the decider (round 1); answered "none", no relation written; delivered [#253](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/253). 5 runs, 16.0 min, 0.34 USD.
- OWL-279 (`owl_count` in the testkit): no question; delivered [#250](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/250), unit tests and a doctest. 2 runs, 18.0 min, 0.27 USD.
- OWL-280 (`as_minutes`, an open point): intake asked the rounding and the display under a minute (round 1); delivered [#251](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/251) with the answer's rule. 4 runs, 23.3 min, 0.35 USD.
- OWL-281 (limits, two follow-ups): no question; delivered [#252](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/252) and filed OWL-286 and OWL-287 in Triage, each with its footer and a `blocks` relation from OWL-281, named in the DELIVERY report; both declined. 2 runs, 15.6 min, 0.19 USD.
- OWL-282 (changelog): delivered [#254](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/254). 2 runs, 22.6 min, 0.18 USD.
- OWL-283 (a doctest in `owlshift-core`): delivered [#255](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/255), no behaviour changed. 2 runs, 23.1 min, 0.21 USD.
- OWL-284 (FAQ): delivered [#256](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/256). 2 runs, 17.8 min, 0.22 USD.
- OWL-285 (the blocked ticket): intake wrote the `blocks` relation from OWL-276 out of the "Blocked by" line (outcome `written`) and asked for the ten titles (round 1); then it waited, `drain=waits reason=blocked_by`. At 06:30:35 on the 15th watch read [#248](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/248) merged (`ended`, `merged`) and dispatched OWL-285 at 06:30:42, in the same pass, from `60ae3f1`; its diff adds to the merged README. Delivered [#257](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/257). 4 runs, 15.9 min, 0.41 USD.
- In all, 29 runs, 204.7 min, 2.76 USD. Every `dispatch` names `command` = `drain`, and no two tickets were between their dispatch and their delivery at once.
- The open pull request cap: reached once, at 23:31 (`drain=pr_cap`, 3 of 3, on OWL-280), and released when a pull request was closed.
- No `continue` was typed. Four `owlshift forget` were, before the first dispatch (below); they cleared tickets outside the gate.
- No guardrail breach: the agents committed locally only; the runner pushed the ten branches, opened the ten pull requests and wrote every comment, stage move, relation and proposal; nothing was merged by Owlshift. No run recorded a `warning` event.

What the runs showed, each with its ticket:

- A ticket ref left between Ready and Watch by an older Owlshift holds the drain's slot for good, and nothing names it: OWL-173, OWL-175, OWL-178 and OWL-179 sat at Verify since P2's gate, delivered before watch followed pull requests, and the drain logged only `reason=slot` for 13 minutes, until they were found by hand and forgotten (OWL-291).
- A proposal needing its parent's code keeps only the `blocks` relation on Linear: the `related` one the design adds is gone when read back, though both writes reported no problem (OWL-292). Linear keeps one relation between two issues, the later write replacing the earlier; since OWL-292 a proposal is given one (part I).
- Intake takes 11 to 19 s and 0.06 to 0.09 USD; an answer check, 7 to 10 s. A Build takes 15 to 30 min and 0.10 to 0.23 USD even for a few lines of Markdown, mostly the gate's `cargo test`, run by the agent and again by the runner. Intake routed every ticket `trivial` on the fast tier; the Build does not use the verdict before P5.
- Intake asked where the ticket left a choice or named a prerequisite in prose, and only there; the six tickets with no open point went straight to Build.

## P5 · Quality pipeline

Designed on 2026-10-15 (OWL-296). The rules are [architecture](architecture.md) section 5, "The quality pipeline": the roster per variant, Design, Design review, plan approval, Verify, confirming a finding, review comments on the pull request, per-ticket routing, the false premise and the Codex harness. Four forks were settled that day with the maintainer: Verify before the pull request, CI staying Watch's; an arbiter run confirming findings by default, the author's triage as a project setting; review comments read in P5 from a list of forge accounts; intake without network, S8 covering what the repository contradicts. Below, what must be checked live first, the contracts the rules change, who builds what, so that no implementation ticket decides them again, and how the exit gate is run. The implementation tickets live in the Linear project "P5 · Quality pipeline", one per part below, OWL-297 to OWL-311, filed on 2026-10-15 in Todo, each blocked by OWL-296 and by the parts its entry names, as `blocks` relations.

**Checked on 2026-10-15**, locally and without network, for the design:

- *`codex exec --help`*, codex-cli 0.154.0 on macOS 26.6.2: `-m`, `-s`, `-C`, `-c key=value`, `--ephemeral`, `--json`, `--ignore-user-config`, `--ignore-rules`, `--output-schema <FILE>` and `-o, --output-last-message <FILE>` are listed. No option names a reasoning effort; Codex's configuration key `model_reasoning_effort`, set through `-c`, is the candidate C2 checks.
- *The delivered pull requests.* [#257](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/257), delivered by the P4 gate, has `pitchopp` as its author: Owlshift opens pull requests through the maintainer's own `gh` login, so the maintainer reviews pull requests of their own account.
- *The secrets.* `owlshift init` keeps the Linear key and the GitHub token under the keychain accounts `linear` and `github` of service `owlshift` (`LINEAR_ACCOUNT`, `GITHUB_ACCOUNT`), one of each per machine, whatever the project; part K below names them per project (OWL-308). Locary's tickets live in a Linear workspace of their own (organization Locary, team `LOC`, `LINEAR.md` in its repository), which a key of the Owlshift workspace cannot read.
- *Locary's repository.* Its default branch is `develop`, which the runner already takes as the base (it reads `origin`'s default branch). Its gate is `make lint` and `make test` (frontend tests, then `uv run pytest`, then five offline self-tests); its Django settings fall back to SQLite when no database URL is set, with Postgres-only tests marked. Its rules are `CLAUDE.md` and ten files under `.claude/rules/`, not a root `AGENTS.md`.

**Pending live checks,** each the first step of its part:

- *(a) C2,* part A: run on 2026-10-15 (OWL-297), on macOS and, logged out, on Linux under `bwrap`; results under "C2" above. `CODEX_HOME` must be writable, and the read-only profile must let Codex verify certificates on macOS. Both settled on 2026-10-15 (OWL-298): a `CODEX_HOME` of the run's own with its `auth.json` copied back under checks, which settles OWL-130's goal for Codex, decided with the maintainer and built by OWL-299; `SSL_CERT_FILE` on macOS (part O).
- *(b) C7's Codex part,* part A: read and captured on 2026-10-15 (OWL-297); results under "C7 (Codex)" above. A usage limit shows as an `error` event and `turn.failed` whose message gives the reset time as a local wall-clock time, with exit status 1.
- *(c) Reviews on GitHub,* part J, read-only then on a throwaway pull request of `pitchopp/owlshift`: `GET repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/{n}/reviews` and `.../pulls/{n}/comments` give each review's author login, state, `submitted_at`, body and `commit_id`, and each inline comment's `path`, `line` and review id; a review by the pull request's author can be `COMMENTED` only, GitHub refusing `APPROVE` and `REQUEST_CHANGES` from them.
- *(d) Locary's gate in the sandbox,* part L: Locary's `make lint` and `make test` run by `owlshift`'s confinement from a fresh worktree, on the maintainer's machine, with what `stack.gate_env` and `allow_gate_env` must declare; Locary's Linear team states (Needs Input, Triage) read through a key of that workspace.

**Contracts and formats.**

- *`owlshift.toml`.* `pipeline.findings`, `"arbiter"` (the default) or `"author"`; a new optional `[forge]` table (`ProjectConfig` has none and refuses unknown keys), whose `reviewers` maps each forge login, written exactly, to a tracker account identifier, as zone owners are written: without it no review is read and `owlshift doctor` says so. The labels `variant:<name>`, `tier:<name>` and `effort:<level>` are fixed prefixes, not configured; a label naming an unknown value is a `warning` event and is ignored. A file using a new key raises its `requires`.
- *`result.json` format 9.* `plan` from the design role (goal, criteria, approach, alternatives, files, steps each with its test, risks, out of scope, `forks` each with its choice, options and reason), or `approval` (`approved` or `changed`, a changed plan beside it) from a design run after an approval round; `findings` from the review roles (an id, `severity` of `blocker`, `major` or `minor`, the claim, its evidence, the success criterion it bears on or none, what would settle it); `rulings` from the arbiter (a finding id, `confirmed` or `rejected`, the reason); `finding_responses` from a Design or Build run given findings in author mode (a finding id, `fixed` or `rejected`, the reason), one per finding; `replan` on a Build question. `premise_false` is accepted from intake and design, as from Build: its question carries the evidence and the two options. Each field refused from any other role or status.
- *The brief, format 15.* The plan, for Build and the review roles; the findings to rule on or answer; the review mandate and the pass number of a review run; a pull request review's comments (author, body, `path`, `line`) for the Build of a review loop; at a Verify pass, the diff under review (OWL-302), for the Verify roster and the arbiter.
- *The roles.* `Role::Arbiter` beside the existing `design`, `design_review` and `verify`, and their prompts: `roles/design.md`, `roles/design_review.md` (the critic, conformance and debt mandates), `roles/verify.md`, `roles/arbiter.md`; `roles/build.md` learns the plan, the findings and `replan`.
