- *The ticket ref.* `plan.json` (contract `TicketPlan`, format 1): the plan, the text's hash, the base commit, and the approval (the decider's comment and the plan's hash) once given. `review.json` (contract `TicketReview`, format 1): per stage and pass, each finding with its reviewer's role, harness and model, its ruling or response, and its outcome (`fixed`, `rejected`, `asked`, `proposed`); and the ids of the pull request reviews answered. `state.json` (format 4): the review passes of the current stage, beside the fix passes and repairs. `questions.json` (format 11): a question's `replan` mark, so the round's resume routes it. `delivery.json` format 5: a push of kind `review`, beside delivery, fix, rebase and correction, and its report's lead line; older formats still read, and an older binary refusing format 5, as each earlier bump did.
- *Marked comments.* `[owlshift] PLAN` on the ticket. The DELIVERY report adds the reviewers that ran (harness, model, stand-in), the findings rejected with their reasons, the residue filed, and the review a delivery answers.
- *Events.* `decision` events with `review` = `pass`, `ruling` or `residue`, and `routing` = `followed` or `not_followed`. Free-form data, so the event format stays 2.
- *The core.* Pure functions over plain data: the stages of each variant from Ready, Design for `standard` and `risky`; the review passes at Design review and at Verify, `MAX_REVIEW_PASSES` = 2, and the one pass after an answered round; the return stage of a round, Design for one asked at Design review, Build for one asked at Verify or for a `replan` on `trivial`; the approval gate (OWL-32); the loop back from Build to Design once a `replan` question is settled, and from Watch to Build on a review; the roster of a variant and its stand-in; the routing resolution in section 9's order (project default, role, verdict with its labels, machine).

**Who builds what.** Each part is blocked by OWL-296 and by the parts named in it.

- **A · Codex live checks.** (OWL-297) Checks (a) and (b), recorded above with their dates; the scrubbed recordings the adapter's contract tests replay. Done on 2026-10-15: `crates/owlshift-adapters/tests/fixtures/codex/`, checked by `tests/codex_fixtures.rs`; the adapter's own `command()` re-records them (OWL-299), as `claude_record.rs` does for Claude Code.
- **O · A read-only confinement profile.** (OWL-298) After A. A profile of the executor's confinement, on `sandbox-exec` and on `bwrap`, with the worktree and the repository's git folder unwritable, and the brief and the harness's result file in the run's own temporary folder; the isolation check unchanged. Used by Codex runs in P5 (architecture, "The Codex harness").
  - *As built* (2026-10-15). A harness asks for it through `SandboxNeeds::read_only`; `RunPaths::read_only` moves the worktree and the git folder from the policy's written folders to its read ones, so `sandbox.rs` is unchanged. The brief and the result go to `run-files` in the run's temporary folder, bound read and written so `bwrap`'s private `/tmp` shows it; `HarnessRun::result_file` names the result, read from there with links refused, the brief's `result_path` not applying; the harness keeps its own files of the run there too, Codex's `CODEX_HOME` among them (OWL-299). Two refusals the design did not name: a brief whose permissions are not read-only, and a harness folder to write that holds or lies in the worktree or the git folder. On macOS only, a read-only harness command gets `SSL_CERT_FILE=/etc/ssl/cert.pem` when the agent environment names none and the file is there. Checked live on 2026-10-15 through `Executor::run` with codex-cli 0.154.0 logged out and a `CODEX_HOME` in `run-files`: under the read-only profile the command got the bundle and reached the API (401 in 15 s); under the write profile, `UnknownIssuer` until the executor's timeout. The isolation check does not compare the worktree's files: the sandbox alone keeps them. Tests: `tests/confinement.rs` (the writes refused and the reads made, on Seatbelt and on the Linux job's `bwrap`; the result read from `run-files` alone; a writing brief refused) and unit tests on the policy, the overlapping folder and the bundle.
- **B · The Codex harness adapter.** (OWL-299) After A and O. The command (`codex exec` with the flags of the architecture's "The Codex harness"), Codex's sandbox off inside the read-only profile, the `--json` stream read for usage and limits, `result.json` written by the runner from `-o`'s message and validated, the effort mapping. `doctor`'s Codex line: a missing or logged-out Codex stays a warning, now saying the critic runs on Claude Code without it, which replaces the condition `not_needed_before_review_roles` left for P5. OWL-131 (`--ignore-user-config` pinned, the trusted-repository MCP check) and OWL-132 (the apps server and `$CODEX_HOME/.env`) follow it, before any Codex run reviews a ticket.
- **C · Contracts.** (OWL-300) The keys and the `[forge]` table, `result.json` format 9, the brief's format 15, `Role::Arbiter`, `TicketPlan`, `TicketReview`, the formats of `state.json`, `questions.json` and `delivery.json` above, and their schemas.
  - *As built* (2026-10-15). The brief's `plan` also reaches the design run that revises or answers it and the arbiter, whose findings cite plan steps; a design brief's `approval` flag marks an approval round and makes the result's `approval` required. The brief's `review` gives a list of `mandates`, a critic alone in its pass holding conformance too. `proposals.json` goes to format 2, since a review loop's run is a `PushKind` it records; format 1 is still read. `FINDINGS_FILE` gave way to `REVIEW_FILE`. The DELIVERY report's new lines and the review push's handling stay G's and J's; `TicketState::review_passes` is carried unchanged by every transition until D gives it its rules.
  - *The diff* (OWL-302, 2026-10-15). A run at a Verify pass is told what it reviews in the brief: `diff`, with `base`, the full commit id the Build started from, and `path`, a file the runner writes with `git diff <base>..HEAD` beside the brief, given as `result_path` is and never tracked. It goes in `review.diff` for the verify role and in `findings.diff` for the arbiter, whose findings to rule on then come from a Verify pass; it is refused for the design review role and with findings to answer or fix. A read-only Claude Code run has no Bash, and the plan's base is not the Build's (the Build starts from the base fetched at its dispatch, and `trivial` has no plan), so the reviewer cannot find the range itself; the diff's text is not put in the brief, whose JSON strings are single lines. Optional in the contract, so the bench's briefs stay as they are: G gives it at every Verify pass, to the verify runs and the arbiter, and Codex's read-only profile (O) must leave the file readable. Format 15 stays: it is not released, so no binary reads it without the field. Rejected: letting read-only git commands through Claude Code's read-only mapping, a guardrail the runner keeps.
- **D · The pipeline in the core.** (OWL-301) After C. The state machine and the functions of "The core" above, but the approval gate, which OWL-32 adds on top, after D.
  - *As built* (2026-10-15).
    - *Review passes.* `TicketState::review_passes` counts the passes ended in the current review loop, Design and Design review or Build and Verify: a `LoopBack` or `Questions` from Design review or Verify ends one. A move inside the loop, a round, a park or a restart keeps the count. Entering a stage of another loop, or of none, resets it: Design review to Build, Verify to Deliver, a `replan` from Build to Design. `review_pass_due` numbers the pass due from 1, as `review.json` does, and `is_last_review_pass` holds from `MAX_REVIEW_PASSES` on. So the pass after an answered round is a last one, and the core refuses a `LoopBack` after a last pass. Rejected: an after-round flag beside the count, a `state.json` change for what the count already says. `with_review_passes` refuses a count above 0 outside the loop's four stages.
    - *Rounds.* `Pipeline::round_return` gives Design for a round asked at Design review and Build for one asked at Verify, and `Questions` uses it. A round waiting at Design review or Verify, as the contracts' fixture holds, is still restored but no longer reached.
    - *`replan`.* Two events, valid at Build only. `ReplanRound`: a round opens for a run that asked a `replan`, asked or decided. `ReplanDecided`: the resolver settled every question of the run. Both go to `Pipeline::replan`, Design, or Build on `trivial`. A fix run's `replan` at Watch is a plain question, Watch's runs keeping P3's rules. Rejected: a round returning to Build and re-routed at its resume from `questions.json`'s mark, a waiting state naming the wrong next stage.
    - *Settling a pass.* `review::settle(stage, pass, findings)` takes the findings that stand, those the arbiter confirmed or the author did not reject, and gives each a fate, `Fix`, `Ask`, `Note` or `Propose`, and the pass an outcome, `Passed`, `Revise` or `Ask`, whose `event()` is `Completed`, `LoopBack` or `Questions`. A noted `minor` keeps no `FindingOutcome` in `review.json`, since nothing settles it.
    - *Approval (OWL-32).* OWL-32 puts the approval round on top of a `Passed` Design review, before its `Completed`, which leaves the loop: a `Questions` at Design review counts a pass and returns to Design, so a changed plan gets its one pass, a last one.
      - *As built* (OWL-32, 2026-10-15). `GatePolicy::plan_gate(pipeline, design_has_fork, plan_sha256, approved_sha256)` gives a `PlanGate`, the only way to build one: whether the plan must be approved, and whether the approval `plan.json` keeps is of this plan's hash, an empty hash never approved; the core compares the hashes, the runner computes them from `Plan::canonical`. `TicketState::apply` refuses every move from Design or Design review to Build in a pipeline with Design review; `TicketState::apply_gated` makes it with the gate: `Completed` at Design review needs it open, and the new `Event::PlanApproved`, a design run answering an approval round `approved`, Design to Build, needs a review pass of the loop behind it and a gate that required the approval and holds it, so a Design back from a `replan` or revising its plan never skips its review. `PassOutcome::design_review_event(gate)` gives the approval round, `Questions`, for a passed pass whose gate is shut. No format changes: `plan.json`'s `approval` is the record. A rule F applies: a plan a design run writes starts unapproved, `plan.json`'s approval dropped, even when it is the approved plan unchanged, and only an `approved` answer records one; so a replanned plan is approved again, as section 5 has it, and the hash check stays on top. Rejected: the approval in `state.json`, a format bump for what `plan.json` keeps; a gate argument to `apply`, whose callers outside the design loop have none to give, which is why the gated path is a method of its own; events the runner picks with nothing for the core to check.
    - *Roster.* `roster::roster(variant, stage, codex_usable)` gives the seats of the table, the critic first, holding conformance when alone, and on Claude Code as a stand-in without Codex. `roster::other_model` picks a reviewer's tier on Claude Code: the deep tier, or the standard tier when the author ran on deep; then the other of the two; then the author's own, flagged `same_model`.
    - *Routing.* `routing::ticket_routing` reads the verdict under the labels' values, which the runner parses (OWL-305), and flags a Codex verdict as not followed. `role_routing` gives each role its layer. `seat_routing` puts the Codex critic on the standard tier and the Claude Code reviewers on `other_model`'s tier, every reviewer on the ticket's effort (architecture section 5, "Per-ticket routing", the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-15). The machine's layer is the roster's stand-in.
    - *Types.* `Effort`, `Routing`, `Severity` and `Mandate` moved to the core's vocabulary, re-exported where the contracts had them, schemas unchanged.
    - *Runner.* The runner's only change is `VisibleStage::after(ReplanRound)`, needs input. Everything else is F's, G's, H's and J's to apply.
- **E · The role prompts.** (OWL-302) After C. `design`, `design_review`, `verify` and `arbiter`, and `build`'s additions (the plan, the findings to answer in author mode, `replan`); each played once by hand on a past Owlshift ticket, the output recorded with the pull request.
  - *As built* (2026-10-15). `roles/design.md`, `roles/design_review.md`, `roles/verify.md` and `roles/arbiter.md`, built into the runner beside the others (`DESIGN_ROLE`, `DESIGN_REVIEW_ROLE`, `VERIFY_ROLE`, `ARBITER_ROLE`) for F and G to run. `roles/build.md` follows a given `plan` step by step, a base newer than the plan's changing nothing by itself, and asks a `replan` question rather than planning around it; it fixes findings in both modes the contract gives Build, `fix` (the Verify fix run) and `answer`, answering the latter in `finding_responses`. `premise_false` from design gives its evidence in `summary`, as Build's does, until I adds the question. The review roles ask no question: what a plan or a diff leaves unclear is a finding. The arbiter rules two findings of one defect alike, the later one's reason naming the earlier, and never rejects one as a duplicate, since each may carry its own severity and criterion. The prompts are harness-neutral: a run that cannot write `result.json` gives it as its last message, as the Codex critic does (B). `tests/roles.rs` pins each prompt against the formats: front matter, the fields and values it writes, the brief fields it reads, and its example validated against a brief of its role. The brief's `diff` is under C's "As built" above. Played on OWL-251 (pull request #238): design at its base, design review (the critic on Codex, conformance on Claude Code), the arbiter on that pass, verify (the critic on Codex) on #238's diff, the arbiter on that pass, and a Verify fix run answering its findings; each result validated against its brief, and the outputs posted on this ticket's pull request.
- **F · Design and Design review in the runner.** (OWL-303) After B, D, E, OWL-131 and OWL-132, so that no Codex run reviews a ticket before its guardrails. The dispatch from Ready to Design, the base fetched there (OWL-274's fast-forward moved) and again at the dispatch to Build; the design run, the PLAN comment and `plan.json`; the roster with its stand-in; the arbiter or the author's responses; the revision loop, its residue round and the pass after it; the approval round, its answer and the design run after it; the Build's plan from the ref; a settled `replan` question sending the ticket back to Design.
- **G · Verify in the runner.** (OWL-304) After F, whose on-demand path it shares. The roster on the diff after the runner's gate and the late-comment re-read, before the push; confirmation; the Verify fix loop; the residue as follow-ups through P4's proposals, or as a question round at Verify for a `blocker` or a finding on a criterion, and the pass after it; the DELIVERY report's new lines.
- **H · Per-ticket routing.** (OWL-305) After C. The verdict's tier and effort, the labels, the roles that follow them, the verdict's harness shown as not followed on the INTAKE comment, the `routing` events.
  - *As built* (2026-10-15). At every dispatch, `do`'s, `continue`'s, the drain's and Watch's fix, correction and rebase runs alike, the runner reads the ticket's `tier:` and `effort:` labels, the prefix and the value without regard to case, and the verdict from the intake record (none for a ticket intake never ran on: the standard tier, no effort), and resolves them through the core's `ticket_routing`. A value that names no tier or effort, and two labels of one prefix that disagree, are each a `warning` event (`label_ignored`, the label, the reason) and are left out; one value repeated counts once. The dispatch then emits one `decision` event, `routing` = `followed` or `not_followed`, with the command, the verdict, the build role's harness, tier, effort and model, and `tier_from` and `effort_from` (`label`, `verdict` or `default`). It is the ticket's routing: a rebase run's own tier stays its `rebase_run` event's. Build, the fix run and the correction run take it; the rebase role takes no effort, on the fast tier for a trivial conflict's first run and on the ticket's tier after. A tier's model is `[models].<tier>.claude`, the harness's own default when the project names none, but `fast` falls back to `standard` as before. The seam is the runner's `routing::Router`, a harness for a core `RunRouting`, one router per harness family: `OnDemand.build` and `OnDemand.rebase` are routers, `rebase_fast` is gone, and F and G route their roles through it with `role_routing` and `seat_routing`. The INTAKE comment adds "Harness not followed" under a verdict naming Codex. Tests: the labels, the models and the router in `routing.rs`; the INTAKE line in `writer.rs`; on the bench, a Build under a verdict with an `effort:` label and an unknown `tier:` one, a Codex verdict, a fix run and a failing rebase on `tier:` and `effort:` labels.
- **I · The false premise.** (OWL-306) After C, E and F, which runs the design and its rounds. `roles/intake.md` and `roles/design.md` on `premise_false`, accepted from both; the round with the evidence and the two options; the record kept as for any round of their stage.
- **J · Review comments on the pull request.** (OWL-307) After G, then check (c). The forge's read of reviews and their comments, for each followed pull request at a pass with no repair due, one more request each; `[forge].reviewers` with the decider rule and doctor's line; the loop back to Build at Watch in the same pass, one Build on the delivered branch for every review due, Verify, the push and the DELIVERY report; the answered ids; the following kept while the ticket is at Build or Verify; its questions.
- **K · Secrets per project.** (OWL-308) The keychain accounts named per project, so a machine serves Owlshift's workspace and Locary's at once; `init` and `--replace-secrets` per project; existing secrets read under their old account until replaced; doctor's lines. Decided on 2026-10-15 (OWL-308), with the maintainer for the first two points:
  - *What names a project.* Its GitHub repository, the owner and name of `origin` as `do` reads them, which already name its folder in the data directory. Each tracker and forge secret is kept under service `owlshift`, account `github.com/<owner>/<repo>/<name>` in lower case (GitHub names ignore case), where `<name>` is the shared account's: `linear`, `linear-app-client-id`, `linear-app-client-secret` and `github`, so `github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/linear`. No `owlshift.toml` key. Two repositories in one Linear workspace store the key twice, and a renamed repository runs `owlshift init` again. Rejected: the tracker's team or workspace, which a GitHub token does not follow and a Markdown project has none of; a name in the project file, which every developer shares.
  - *The Linear app goes with the key.* The app's client ID and secret are the project's too, since an app belongs to one workspace. The tracker set, the key and the app's pair, is read whole from one place: the project's accounts when any of the three is stored there, otherwise the shared ones when any of those is, otherwise the project's, which then hold nothing. So a project with its own key and no app of its own writes through its key, never through another workspace's app. The GitHub token is resolved the same way, on its own. Each account is read at most once per command.
  - *The shared accounts.* The accounts every project of a machine used before OWL-308 are still read for a project that has none of its own, so an existing install keeps working, and Owlshift never deletes them. `do`, `continue` and `watch` say on their error output, once, which set comes from a shared account, naming the accounts and the repository, never a value. The Claude Code token for agent runs (`claude-agent`) stays one per machine: a model login, not a tracker or forge secret.
  - *`init`.* It stores under the project's accounts, read from `origin`; it asks for a project's secret even when a shared one is in use, and an empty answer keeps the shared one. `--replace-secrets` replaces the project's. The app's pair is asked once the project has its own key, saying that a shared app is not used with it. Without a GitHub `origin`, `init` stores the Claude Code token, names the project's secrets as not stored and why, and exits 1.
  - *`doctor`.* A `secrets` line names the account each tracker and forge secret is read from: a warning, which does not block, when one is shared, whose fix is `owlshift init --replace-secrets` in the repository; a failure without a GitHub token, as `do` refuses then, without a GitHub `origin`, or when the keychain cannot be read. The tracker lines name the resolved accounts, and the advice to delete the app's two entries to write through the key is given for the project's own entries only, never for shared ones another project may use.
  - *The platforms.* The account names hold `/` and `.`: the Windows store (`windows-native-keyring-store` 1.1.0) checks their length only, at most 513 characters for the user name, and the Secret Service keeps them as attribute strings; a name here is at most about 170 characters. Read on 2026-10-15 in the crates' sources; the tests use the in-memory store on the three platforms, and the round trip through the macOS Keychain on such a name is a live check (`system_keychain_round_trip`, ignored).
- **L · Locary's adoption.** (OWL-309) After K, then check (d). Locary's `owlshift.toml` (team `LOC`, its states, `admitters`, `[forge].reviewers`, `stack.gate`, `stack.rules` naming `CLAUDE.md` and its rules), proposed to Locary through its own process; its secrets through K; the Linear app of D8 on its workspace or the key with doctor's warning; `owlshift doctor` clean on it.
- **M · The scenarios.** (OWL-310) After F, G, H, I, J and OWL-32. S8, S9's findings and review passes, S17's routing part, each variant walked end to end, plan approval given and refused, a `replan`, and a review loop, played by the test bench on the Markdown tracker with the fake harness and the bench's fake GitHub, as acceptance.
- **N · The exit gate** (OWL-311), below. After M, L, OWL-131 and OWL-132.

## P5 tasks & exit gate

The tasks are the parts above, OWL-297 to OWL-311, in the Linear project "P5 · Quality pipeline", with OWL-32 (after D), OWL-131 and OWL-132 (after B).

**Exit gate.** Five pull requests judged at least as good as `/parallel` output by the maintainer; first Locary tickets delivered. Run by the maintainer, as P4's was, once M passes:

1. *Before.* Codex logged in and `owlshift doctor` clean on both projects; OWL-131 and OWL-132 merged; Locary adopted (part L).
2. *The tickets.* Five real tickets at least, two of them Locary's, drained by `owlshift watch --drain`, each through the stages of its variant. Among them: one `standard` with a design review, one `risky` whose plan approval was asked and given, one where a finding was confirmed and fixed, and one the maintainer's review on its pull request sent back to Build. A case the backlog does not bring is staged, as P2 staged its round 2.
3. *Judged.* For each pull request, the maintainer compares it with `/parallel`'s output on tickets of like size, on five points: it does what the ticket asks and nothing more; the plan's approach holds; the tests cover the criteria; every finding was fixed or rejected for a stated reason; the review it took the maintainer (comments, fixes after delivery). The verdict, at least as good or not, with one line why, is recorded below. Five at least as good pass the gate; one that is not is recorded, and its cause filed as a ticket.
4. *Recorded here,* as P4's runs are: per ticket, its variant and routing, runs per stage, minutes and cost; the reviewers that ran, with any stand-in; findings raised, confirmed and rejected, and the review passes; approvals asked and given; the review loops; the follow-ups proposed; and that there was no guardrail breach (agents committed locally only, the runner wrote every comment and push, nothing was merged by Owlshift).
